Table 4.
Harmonic 2-cochains associated to the SD & ASD Weyl curvature modules

3.4.
Twistor XXO-structures as correspondence spaces

The construction of twistor XXO-structures discussed in Section
2.2
can be described as a Cartan-geometric correspondence space construction
[
11
]
. This has the advantage that it allows us to locally characterize these structures in terms of a curvature condition.

Given nested parabolic subgroups
P\subset Q\subset G
, the correspondence space construction is a natural construction that assigns to any parabolic geometry of type
({\mathfrak{g}},Q)
a parabolic geometry of type
({\mathfrak{g}},P)
. Starting with
(\pi:{\mathcal{G}}\to M,\omega)
of type
({\mathfrak{g}},Q)
, one forms the orbit space

(3.7)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}M:={\mathcal{G}}/P={\mathcal{G}}\times_{Q}(Q/P).

The natural projection
{\mathcal{G}}\to{\mathcal{C}}M
is a
P
-principal bundle and
({\mathcal{G}}\to{\mathcal{C}}M,\omega)
is a Cartan geometry of type
({\mathfrak{g}},P)
(referred to as the
correspondence space
). If
Q/P
is connected, then this construction defines an equivalence of categories between Cartan geometries of type
({\mathfrak{g}},Q)
and a subcategory of Cartan geometries of type
({\mathfrak{g}},P)
, see
[
12
, Prop. 1.5.13]
. If
({\mathcal{G}}\to M,\omega)
is a regular, normal parabolic geometry, then
({\mathcal{G}}\to{\mathcal{C}}M,\omega)
is automatically normal, but regularity is in general not preserved.

Given a correspondence space
({\mathcal{G}}\to{\mathcal{C}}M,\omega)
, the vertical bundle
V{\mathcal{C}}M
of
{\mathcal{C}}M\to M
corresponds to
\mathfrak{q}/{\mathfrak{p}}\subset{\mathfrak{g}}/{\mathfrak{p}}
and the curvature has the property that
\kappa(X,\cdot)=0
for any
X\in\mathfrak{q}/{\mathfrak{p}}
.
It can be shown that this curvature condition locally characterizes correspondence spaces. For regular, normal parabolic geometries, there is an even more efficient characterization in terms of harmonic curvature. Theorems 2.7 and 3.3 in
[
11
]
show that a regular, normal parabolic geometry of type
({\mathfrak{g}},P)
is locally isomorphic to the correspondence space of a normal parabolic geometry of type
({\mathfrak{g}},Q)
if and only if its harmonic curvature has the property that
\kappa_{H}(X,\cdot)=0
for any
X\in\mathfrak{q}
.

Here we apply these results to the nested parabolic subgroups
\widetilde{P}\subset\widetilde{Q}\subset\mathrm{SL}(4,{\mathbb{R}})
defined in Section
3.2
.
The first observation is the following:

Lemma 3.1
.

The twistor XXO-structure on the bundle of SD totally null 2-planes over a 4D split-conformal structure can be naturally identified with the XXO-structure on the correspondence space
{\mathcal{C}}M={\mathcal{G}}\times_{\widetilde{Q}}\widetilde{Q}/\widetilde{P}
.

Proof.

Let
({\mathcal{G}}\to M,\omega)
be a normal parabolic geometry of type
(\mathrm{SL}(4,{\mathbb{R}}),\widetilde{Q})
with associated oriented split-conformal structure
(M,[\mathsf{g}])
. Recall the identification of
\mathfrak{sl}(4,{\mathbb{R}})/\widetilde{\mathfrak{q}}
with the space of
2\times 2
matrices
\mathrm{M}_{2}{\mathbb{R}}
, embedded as the bottom-left
2\times 2
block in
\mathfrak{sl}(4,{\mathbb{R}})
. The
\widetilde{Q}
-action on this space, as discussed in Section
3.2
, defines a surjection from
\widetilde{Q}
onto the connected component of the identity of the group
\mathrm{CSO}_{0}(2,2)
of linear conformal transformations of
\mathfrak{sl}(4,{\mathbb{R}})/\widetilde{\mathfrak{q}}
. This action preserves the two distinguished sets of SD/ASD totally null
2
-planes in
\mathfrak{sl}(4,{\mathbb{R}})/\widetilde{\mathfrak{q}}
and is transitive on them.
It is easy to see that the set of maps in
\mathrm{M}_{2}{\mathbb{R}}
that vanish on the line
\left\langle e_{1}\right\rangle
through the first basis vector in the standard representation of
\mathfrak{sl}(4,{\mathbb{R}})
defines such a totally null
2
-plane and that its stabilizer in
\widetilde{Q}
is exactly the subgroup
\widetilde{P}\subset\widetilde{Q}
.

This provides the identification
\Phi:{\mathcal{C}}M={\mathcal{G}}\times_{\widetilde{Q}}\widetilde{Q}/\widetilde{P}\cong{\mathbb{T}}^{+}(M)
. Since the normal conformal geometry
({\mathcal{G}}\to M,\omega)
is torsion-free, the geometry
({\mathcal{G}}\to{\mathcal{C}}M,\omega)
is regular and thus has an induced XXO-structure. Moreover, via the Cartan connection,
T{\mathcal{C}}M\cong{\mathcal{G}}\times_{\widetilde{P}}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}/\widetilde{{\mathfrak{p}}})
and the map
\pi_{*}:T{\mathcal{C}}M\to TM
corresponds to the projection
\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}/\widetilde{{\mathfrak{p}}}\to\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}/\widetilde{\mathfrak{q}}
. Using this, it is straightforward to see that
\Phi
is indeed an isomorphism of XXO-structures.
∎

The general results on correspondence spaces then immediately lead to the following:

Proposition 3.1
.

An XXO-structure is locally isomorphic to the twistor XXO-structure on the bundle of SD totally null 2-planes over a 4D split-conformal structure if and only if it has vanishing harmonic curvature component
\mathcal{T}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
3.1
, the line bundle
\ell
of the XXO-structure corresponds to the vertical bundle
V{\mathcal{C}}M
of
{\mathcal{C}}M\to M
. From Table
3
, we see that the harmonic curvature of an XXO-structure vanishes upon insertion of any element of
\ell
if and only if
{\mathcal{T}}\equiv 0
. Thus the result is an immediate consequence of Theorems 2.7 and 3.3 in
[
11
]
.
∎

Remark 3.2
.

Having identified the twistor XXO-structure as the underlying structure of a correspondence space, this also allows us to relate the components of the harmonic curvatures
\kappa_{H}^{{\mathcal{C}}M}
and
\kappa_{H}^{M}
(see Table
3
) via

(3.8)

\displaystyle\kappa_{H}^{{\mathcal{C}}M}=\mathrm{pr}\circ\kappa_{H}^{M}:{\mathcal{G}}\to H_{2}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{q}}_{+},\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}})/((\widetilde{\mathfrak{p}}_{+}\cap\widetilde{\mathfrak{q}}_{0})\cdot H_{2}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{q}}_{+},\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}))\,\subset\,H_{2}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{p}}_{+},\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}).

In this way, we recover the relationship between the Weyl tensor
{\mathcal{W}}^{+}
and the harmonic curvature component
{\mathcal{I}}
via parabolic geometry machinery, which was obtained by direct computation in Lemma
2.3
. Similarly,
{\mathcal{W}}^{-}
can be related to
{\mathcal{S}}
.

3.5.
Symmetries and gaps

The (infinitesimal) symmetry algebra of each of the three structures of interest is formulated naturally via the Lie derivative, e.g. for an XXO-structure
(\ell,{\mathcal{D}})
on a 5-manifold
N
, a symmetry is a vector field
{\mathbf{X}}\in{\mathfrak{X}}(N)
such that
{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathbf{X}}\ell\subset\ell
and
{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathbf{X}}{\mathcal{D}}\subset{\mathcal{D}}
. By the aforementioned equivalence of categories statements, we may equivalently study the symmetry of the corresponding Cartan geometry.

Given any Cartan geometry
({\mathcal{G}}\to M,\omega)
of type
({\mathfrak{g}},P)
, its symmetry algebra is

(3.9)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{inf}({\mathcal{G}},\omega):=\{\xi\in{\mathfrak{X}}({\mathcal{G}})^{P}:{\mathcal{L}}_{\xi}\omega=0\}.

Since
\omega
is in particular a coframing, then
{\rm dim}(\mathfrak{inf}({\mathcal{G}},\omega))\leq{\rm dim}({\mathcal{G}})={\rm dim}({\mathfrak{g}})
, with equality realized on the homogeneous model
(G\to G/P,\omega_{G})
. For parabolic geometries, equality is
locally uniquely
realized by this model. The
submaximal
symmetry dimension
{\mathfrak{S}}
is the next realizable symmetry dimension below
{\rm dim}({\mathfrak{g}})
, among all regular normal parabolic geometries of type
({\mathfrak{g}},P)
, and often there is a significant symmetry gap
[
19
]
.

Cartan
[
13
]
showed that
{\mathfrak{S}}=7
for
(2,3,5)
-distributions. For 4D split-conformal structures and XXO-structures, we have
{\mathfrak{S}}=9
. (See
[
19
, Table 12]
for
G=A_{3}
and
P=P_{1}
or
P_{1,2}
respectively.) In particular, we have the following:

Proposition 3.2
.

The symmetry algebra of a non-integrable XXO-structure has dimension
\leq 9
.

Remark 3.3
.

Recall that symmetries of
[\mathsf{g}]
lift to symmetries of the twistor distribution
{\mathcal{D}}
.
Together with the fact that the submaximal symmetry dimension of a
(2,3,5)
distribution is
7
, this immediately implies that the twistor distribution
{\mathcal{D}}
of a conformal structure
[\mathsf{g}]
whose conformal symmetry algebra is at least 8-dimensional is necessarily
\mathrm{G}_{2}
-symmetric.

4.
Abstract classification

4.1.
Classification problem for Lie-theoretic models

A locally homogeneous XXO-structure is completely encoded by a
Lie-theoretic
model
({\mathfrak{f}},{\mathfrak{f}}^{0};{\mathfrak{f}}_{\ell},{\mathfrak{f}}_{\mathcal{D}})
: namely, its symmetry algebra
{\mathfrak{f}}
and isotropy subalgebra
{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}\subset{\mathfrak{f}}
at a generic basepoint
o\in N
, and
{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
-invariant subspaces
{\mathfrak{f}}_{\ell},{\mathfrak{f}}_{\mathcal{D}}\subset{\mathfrak{f}}/{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
corresponding to
\ell,\mathcal{D}\subset TN
at
o
.

Now assume that
{\mathcal{D}}
is
(2,3,5)
and has vanishing Cartan tensor
{\mathcal{Q}}\equiv 0
, so that
{\mathcal{D}}
has
{\mathfrak{g}}=\mathsf{Lie}(G_{2})
symmetry. Let
({\mathcal{G}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi}}{{\to}}N,\omega)
be the parabolic geometry of type
({\mathfrak{g}}=\mathsf{Lie}(G_{2}),P)
associated to
{\mathcal{D}}
. Since
{\mathcal{Q}}\equiv 0
, then any
u\in\pi^{-1}(o)\subset{\mathcal{G}}
,
\omega_{u}
restricts to a Lie algebra injection
{\mathfrak{f}}\hookrightarrow{\mathfrak{g}}
. From Section
3.2
, recall that
{\mathfrak{g}}
admits the (depth 3)
(2,3,5)
-filtration

{\mathfrak{g}}={\mathfrak{g}}^{-3}\supset...\supset{\mathfrak{g}}^{3}
with stabilizer
P\subset\text{Aut}({\mathfrak{g}})
, and so
{\mathfrak{f}}
inherits a filtration of the form

(4.1)

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{f}}={\mathfrak{f}}^{-3}\supset{\mathfrak{f}}^{-2}\supset{\mathfrak{f}}^{-1}\supset{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}\supset{\mathfrak{f}}^{1}\supset{\mathfrak{f}}^{2}\supset{\mathfrak{f}}^{3}.

The
{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
here agrees with that above, being the isotropy subalgebra at
o
. Moreover,

(4.2)

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{f}}_{\mathcal{D}}={\mathfrak{f}}^{-1}/{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}\cong{\mathcal{D}}_{o},\quad\mbox{and}\quad{\mathfrak{f}}_{\ell}\oplus{\mathfrak{f}}_{{\mathcal{D}}}={\mathfrak{f}}^{-2}/{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}\cong[{\mathcal{D}},{\mathcal{D}}]_{o}.

Henceforth, we will abuse notation and write simply
(\ell,{\mathcal{D}})
in the abstract setting in place of
({\mathfrak{f}}_{\ell},{\mathfrak{f}}_{\mathcal{D}})
.

Define the associated-graded Lie algebra
{\mathfrak{s}}=\mathrm{gr}({\mathfrak{f}})
, i.e.
{\mathfrak{s}}_{i}={\mathfrak{f}}^{i}/{\mathfrak{f}}^{i+1}
. Since the filtration on
{\mathfrak{g}}
comes from a grading
{\mathfrak{g}}={\mathfrak{g}}_{-3}\oplus...\oplus{\mathfrak{g}}_{3}
, then we can identify
{\mathfrak{s}}
with a graded Lie subalgebra of
{\mathfrak{g}}
. Transitivity of
{\mathfrak{f}}
implies
\mathrm{gr}_{-}({\mathfrak{f}})={\mathfrak{g}}_{-}
, and if we assume that
{\mathfrak{f}}
is multiply-transitive, then
{\rm dim}({\mathfrak{f}}^{0})\geq 1
. The condition that the harmonic curvature component
{\mathcal{T}}
vanish can be understood as a condition on the data
({\mathfrak{f}},{\mathfrak{f}}^{0};\ell,{\mathcal{D}})
. (See for instance Example
A.1
in Appendix
A
.)

Definition 4.1
.

We say that the data
({\mathfrak{f}},{\mathfrak{f}}^{0};\ell,{\mathcal{D}})
is an
admissible
(Lie-theoretic) model if:

(X.1)

{\mathfrak{f}}\hookrightarrow{\mathfrak{g}}
is a filtered Lie subalgebra with
{\mathfrak{g}}
equipped with the
(2,3,5)
-filtration and
{\mathfrak{f}}
equipped with the induced filtration such that
{\mathfrak{f}}^{i}={\mathfrak{g}}^{i}\cap{\mathfrak{f}}
;

(X.2)

\mathrm{gr}_{-}({\mathfrak{f}})={\mathfrak{g}}_{-}
and
{\rm dim}({\mathfrak{f}}^{0})\geq 1
;

(X.3)

{\mathcal{D}}={\mathfrak{f}}^{-1}/{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
, and
\ell\subset{\mathfrak{f}}^{-2}/{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
is an
{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
-invariant line such that
\ell\cap{\mathcal{D}}=0
;

(X.4)

{\mathcal{T}}\equiv 0
;

(X.5)

it is a maximal element (among the set of those satisfying (X.1)-(X.4)) with respect to the natural partial ordering.

For (X.5), the partial ordering is given by declaring
({\mathfrak{f}},{\mathfrak{f}}^{0};\ell,{\mathcal{D}})\leq(\widetilde{\mathfrak{f}},\widetilde{\mathfrak{f}}^{0};\widetilde{\ell},\widetilde{\mathcal{D}})
if there is an
embedding
of filtered Lie algebras
{\mathfrak{f}}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{\mathfrak{f}}
inducing an isomorphism
{\mathfrak{f}}^{-2}/{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}\cong\widetilde{{\mathfrak{f}}}^{-2}/\widetilde{{\mathfrak{f}}}^{0}
mapping
\ell
to
\widetilde{\ell}
. The parabolic subgroup
P
naturally acts on admissible
({\mathfrak{f}},{\mathfrak{f}}^{0};\ell,{\mathcal{D}})
via the adjoint action, e.g.
{\mathfrak{f}}\mapsto{\rm Ad}_{p}{\mathfrak{f}}
,
\forall p\in P
, so we view
P
as the (initial)
structure group
for our problem:

Classify admissible
({\mathfrak{f}},{\mathfrak{f}}^{0};\ell,{\mathcal{D}})
up to the
P
-action.

Here,
P
will be used to bring
{\mathfrak{f}}
into specific (canonical) forms. In doing so, it will be successively reduced, so we will refer to these (reduced) subgroups as
residual structure groups
. We say that the filtered Lie subalgebra
{\mathfrak{f}}\subset{\mathfrak{g}}
is a
filtered deformation
of
{\mathfrak{s}}=\mathrm{gr}({\mathfrak{f}})\subset{\mathfrak{g}}
. It will be convenient to use the following notation. Suppose that
x\in{\mathfrak{g}}^{i}\backslash{\mathfrak{g}}^{i+1}
, so
x=x_{i}+x_{i+1}+...
with
x_{i}\neq 0
and
x_{j}\in{\mathfrak{g}}_{j}
for all
j\geq i
. For
x
, we write
\mathrm{gr}_{i}(x)=x_{i}
for its
leading part
, while the terms of higher homogeneity
x_{i+1}+...
will be referred to as its
tails
.

4.2.
The simple Lie algebra
{\mathfrak{g}}=\mathsf{Lie}(G_{2})

Let us introduce a convenient basis of
{\mathfrak{g}}=\mathsf{Lie}(G_{2})
(adapted to the root space decomposition), pictured on the
G_{2}
root diagram in Figure
1
.

\mathfrak{g}_{-3}
\mathfrak{g}_{-2}
\mathfrak{g}_{-1}
\mathfrak{g}_{0}
\mathfrak{g}_{1}
\mathfrak{g}_{2}
\mathfrak{g}_{3}
\mathsf{Z}_{1},
\mathsf{Z}_{2}
e_{10}
f_{10}
e_{01}
f_{01}
f_{31}
e_{31}
f_{32}
e_{32}
e_{21}
f_{21}
e_{11}
f_{11}

Figure 1.
A basis of
{\mathfrak{g}}=\mathsf{Lie}(G_{2})
adapted to the root space decomposition

The generators
\mathsf{Z}_{i},f_{ij},e_{ij}
in Figure
1
are obtained by differentiation with respect to
z_{i},a_{ij},b_{ij}
of the following
7\times 7
matrix realization of
{\mathfrak{g}}
, as presented in
[
25
]
.

\displaystyle\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{c}\begin{pmatrix}2z_{1}+z_{2}&b_{10}&b_{11}&\sqrt{2}\,b_{21}&b_{31}&b_{32}&0\\
a_{10}&z_{1}+z_{2}&b_{01}&\sqrt{2}\,b_{11}&-b_{21}&0&-b_{32}\\
a_{11}&a_{01}&z_{1}&-\sqrt{2}\,b_{10}&0&b_{21}&-b_{31}\\
\sqrt{2}\,a_{21}&\sqrt{2}\,a_{11}&-\sqrt{2}\,a_{10}&0&\sqrt{2}\,b_{10}&-\sqrt{2}\,b_{11}&-\sqrt{2}\,b_{21}\\
a_{31}&-a_{21}&0&\sqrt{2}\,a_{10}&-z_{1}&-b_{01}&-b_{11}\\
a_{32}&0&a_{21}&-\sqrt{2}\,a_{11}&-a_{01}&-z_{1}-z_{2}&-b_{10}\\
0&-a_{32}&-a_{31}&-\sqrt{2}\,a_{21}&-a_{11}&-a_{10}&-2z_{1}-z_{2}\end{pmatrix}\end{array}.

For
i,j\geq 0
, we have root vectors
e_{ij}
(
raising operators
) and
f_{ij}
(
lowering operators
) lying in the root spaces for
i\alpha_{1}+j\alpha_{2}
and
-i\alpha_{1}-j\alpha_{2}
respectively (where
\alpha_{1}
and
\alpha_{2}
are the simple roots). The
grading element

\mathsf{Z}_{1}
gives rise to the grading of
{\mathfrak{g}}
indicated in Figure
1
. Namely,
{\mathfrak{g}}_{i}
is the
{\rm ad}_{\mathsf{Z}_{1}}
-eigenspace with eigenvalue
i
, and
[\mathsf{Z}_{1},e_{ij}]=ie_{ij}
while
[\mathsf{Z}_{1},f_{ij}]=-if_{ij}
. The full commutator table for
{\mathfrak{g}}
is given in Table
5
.

\begin{array}[]{c|cccccccccccccc}[\cdot,\cdot]&\mathsf{Z}_{1}&\mathsf{Z}_{2}&e_{01}&e_{10}&e_{11}&e_{21}&e_{31}&e_{32}&f_{01}&f_{10}&f_{11}&f_{21}&f_{31}&f_{32}\\
\hline\cr\mathsf{Z}_{1}&\cdot&\cdot&\cdot&e_{10}&e_{11}&2e_{21}&3e_{31}&3e_{32}&\cdot&-f_{10}&-f_{11}&-2f_{21}&-3f_{31}&-3f_{32}\\
\mathsf{Z}_{2}&&\cdot&e_{01}&\cdot&e_{11}&e_{21}&e_{31}&2e_{32}&-f_{01}&\cdot&-f_{11}&-f_{21}&-f_{31}&-2f_{32}\\
e_{01}&&&\cdot&-e_{11}&\cdot&\cdot&e_{32}&\cdot&-\mathsf{Z}_{1}+2\mathsf{Z}_{2}&\cdot&f_{10}&\cdot&\cdot&-f_{31}\\
e_{10}&&&&\cdot&2e_{21}&-3e_{31}&\cdot&\cdot&\cdot&2\mathsf{Z}_{1}-3\mathsf{Z}_{2}&-3f_{01}&-2f_{11}&f_{21}&\cdot\\
e_{11}&&&&&\cdot&3e_{32}&\cdot&\cdot&e_{10}&-3e_{01}&-\mathsf{Z}_{1}+3\mathsf{Z}_{2}&2f_{10}&\cdot&-f_{21}\\
e_{21}&&&&&&\cdot&\cdot&\cdot&\cdot&-2e_{11}&2e_{10}&\mathsf{Z}_{1}&-f_{10}&f_{11}\\
e_{31}&&&&&&&\cdot&\cdot&\cdot&e_{21}&\cdot&-e_{10}&\mathsf{Z}_{1}-\mathsf{Z}_{2}&f_{01}\\
e_{32}&&&&&&&&\cdot&-e_{31}&\cdot&-e_{21}&e_{11}&e_{01}&\mathsf{Z}_{2}\\
f_{01}&&&&&&&&&\cdot&f_{11}&\cdot&\cdot&-f_{32}&\cdot\\
f_{10}&&&&&&&&&&\cdot&-2f_{21}&3f_{31}&\cdot&\cdot\\
f_{11}&&&&&&&&&&&\cdot&-3f_{32}&\cdot&\cdot\\
f_{21}&&&&&&&&&&&&\cdot&\cdot&\cdot\\
f_{31}&&&&&&&&&&&&&\cdot&\cdot\\
f_{32}&&&&&&&&&&&&&&\cdot\\
\end{array}

Table 5.
Bracket relations for
{\mathfrak{g}}=\mathsf{Lie}(G_{2})

4.3.
Isotropy constraints

Lemma 4.2
.

Let
({\mathfrak{f}},{\mathfrak{f}}^{0};\ell,{\mathcal{D}})
be an admissible model. Then
{\mathfrak{f}}^{1}=0
,
{\rm dim}({\mathfrak{f}}^{0})\leq 4
, and
{\rm dim}({\mathfrak{f}})\leq 9
.

Proof.

Let
X\in{\mathfrak{f}}^{1}\subseteq{\mathfrak{g}}^{1}=\langle e_{10},e_{11},e_{21},e_{31},e_{32}\rangle
, so
X_{1}=\mathrm{gr}_{1}(X)=ae_{10}+be_{11}
. Let
L\in\ell
with
f_{21}=\mathrm{gr}_{-2}(L)
. But then

(4.4)

\displaystyle\mathrm{gr}([X,L])=[\mathrm{gr}_{1}(X),\mathrm{gr}_{-2}(L)]=[X_{1},f_{21}]=-2af_{11}+2bf_{10}.

We know that
\ell
must be invariant under
X\in{\mathfrak{f}}^{1}\subseteq{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
, so this forces
a=b=0
, i.e.
X\in{\mathfrak{f}}^{2}
. Thus,
{\mathfrak{s}}=\mathrm{gr}({\mathfrak{f}})
has
{\mathfrak{s}}_{1}=0
. Since the Lie bracket induces
{\mathfrak{g}}_{0}
-module isomorphisms
{\mathfrak{g}}_{2}\times{\mathfrak{g}}_{-1}\to{\mathfrak{g}}_{1}
and
{\mathfrak{g}}_{3}\times{\mathfrak{g}}_{-2}\to{\mathfrak{g}}_{1}
, then from
{\mathfrak{s}}_{-}={\mathfrak{g}}_{-}
(by homogeneity) and
{\mathfrak{s}}_{1}=0
, we conclude that
{\mathfrak{s}}_{2}=0
and
{\mathfrak{s}}_{3}=0
. Hence,
{\mathfrak{f}}^{1}=0
and since
{\mathfrak{s}}_{0}=\mathrm{gr}({\mathfrak{f}}^{0})\subseteq{\mathfrak{g}}_{0}\cong\mathfrak{gl}_{2}
, then
{\rm dim}({\mathfrak{f}}^{0})\leq 4
.
∎

In Section
4.4
, we establish the complete classification of complex admissible
({\mathfrak{f}},{\mathfrak{f}}^{0};\ell,{\mathcal{D}})
, while Section
4.5
will treat real forms.

4.4.
Complex classification

Theorem 4.3
.

The complete classification of complex admissible
({\mathfrak{f}},{\mathfrak{f}}^{0};\ell,{\mathcal{D}})
is given in Table
6
. Moreover, the models
\mathsf{M7}_{\mathsf{a}}
and
\mathsf{M7}_{\mathsf{b}}
are isomorphic iff
\mathsf{b}^{2}=\mathsf{a}^{2}
.

\begin{array}[]{|c|c|l|l|l|}\hline\cr\mbox{Label}&{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}&{\mathfrak{f}}^{-1}/{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}&{\mathfrak{f}}^{-2}/{\mathfrak{f}}^{-1}&{\mathfrak{f}}^{-3}/{\mathfrak{f}}^{-2}\\
\hline\cr\hline\cr\mathsf{M9}&\begin{array}[]{c}e_{01}\\
\mathsf{Z}_{1},\mathsf{Z}_{2}\\
f_{01}\end{array}&\begin{array}[]{l@{\,}l}X_{1}&=f_{10}\\
X_{2}&=f_{11}\end{array}&\begin{array}[]{l@{\,}l}L=X_{3}&=f_{21}\\
\end{array}&\begin{array}[]{l@{\,}l}X_{4}&=f_{31}\\
X_{5}&=f_{32}\end{array}\\
\hline\cr\mathsf{M8}&\begin{array}[]{c}e_{01}\\
\mathsf{H}\\
f_{01}\end{array}&\begin{array}[]{l@{\,}l}X_{1}&=f_{10}+e_{32}\\
X_{2}&=f_{11}+e_{31}\end{array}&\begin{array}[]{l@{\,}l}L=X_{3}&=f_{21}+e_{21}\end{array}&\begin{array}[]{l@{\,}l}X_{4}&=f_{31}+e_{11}\\
X_{5}&=f_{32}+e_{10}\end{array}\\
\hline\cr\mathsf{M7}_{\mathsf{a}}&\begin{array}[]{c}\mathsf{Z}_{2}\\
f_{01}\end{array}&\begin{array}[]{l@{\,}l}X_{1}&=f_{10}+a\mathsf{Z}_{1}+e_{10}\\
X_{2}&=f_{11}\end{array}&\begin{array}[]{l@{\,}l}L=X_{3}&=f_{21}\end{array}&\begin{array}[]{l@{\,}l}X_{4}&=f_{31}\\
X_{5}&=f_{32}\end{array}\\
\hline\cr\mathsf{M6S}&\mathsf{H}&\begin{array}[]{l@{\,}l}X_{1}&=f_{10}+e_{11}\\
X_{2}&=f_{11}+e_{10}\end{array}&\begin{array}[]{l@{\,}l}L=X_{3}&=f_{21}+e_{21}\end{array}&\begin{array}[]{l@{\,}l}X_{4}&=f_{31}+e_{32}\\
X_{5}&=f_{32}+e_{31}\end{array}\\
\hline\cr\mathsf{M6N}&f_{01}&\begin{array}[]{l@{\,}l}X_{1}&=f_{10}+2\mathsf{H}+e_{01}+e_{32}\\
X_{2}&=f_{11}-\mathsf{H}+e_{31}\end{array}&\begin{array}[]{l@{\,}l}X_{3}&=f_{21}+3\mathsf{H}+e_{21}\\
L&=X_{3}+3X_{2}\end{array}&\begin{array}[]{l@{\,}l}X_{4}&=f_{31}+2\mathsf{H}-e_{01}+e_{11}\\
X_{5}&=f_{32}-\mathsf{H}+e_{10}\end{array}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

Table 6.
Complete classification of complex admissible
({\mathfrak{f}},{\mathfrak{f}}^{0};\ell,{\mathcal{D}})
. Here,
\ell=\langle L\rangle\,{\rm mod}\ {\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
and
{\mathcal{D}}=\langle X_{1},X_{2}\rangle\,{\rm mod}\ {\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
. (Notation:
\mathsf{H}=[e_{01},f_{01}]=-\mathsf{Z}_{1}+2\mathsf{Z}_{2}
.)

4.4.1.
Classification strategy

Focus first on
{\mathfrak{s}}=\mathrm{gr}({\mathfrak{f}})
. Since
{\mathfrak{s}}_{-}={\mathfrak{g}}_{-}
, it suffices to determine
{\mathfrak{s}}_{0}=\mathrm{gr}({\mathfrak{f}}^{0})\subseteq{\mathfrak{g}}_{0}
, where
{\mathfrak{g}}_{0}\cong\mathfrak{gl}_{2}\cong{\mathbb{C}}\oplus\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
. The isomorphism
{\mathfrak{g}}_{0}\cong\mathfrak{gl}_{2}
is given by
x\mapsto{\rm ad}_{x}|_{{\mathfrak{g}}_{-1}}
, expressed as a
2\times 2
matrix in the basis
\{f_{10},f_{11}\}
. In particular, this identifies
\mathsf{Z}_{1},f_{01},\mathsf{H},e_{01}
respectively with

(4.5)

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}-1&0\\
0&-1\end{pmatrix},\quad\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
1&0\end{pmatrix},\quad\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&-1\end{pmatrix},\quad\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
0&0\end{pmatrix},

where

(4.6)

\displaystyle\mathsf{H}:=[e_{01},f_{01}]=-\mathsf{Z}_{1}+2\mathsf{Z}_{2}.

The
P
-action induces an action by
G_{0}\cong\mathsf{GL}_{2}
on
{\mathfrak{g}}_{0}
, and up to
\mathsf{GL}_{2}
-conjugacy, the (non-zero) subalgebras of
\mathfrak{gl}_{2}
are given in Table
7
. (Here,
*
denotes arbitrary values, while
\lambda,\lambda_{i}\in{\mathbb{C}}
are fixed.)

\begin{array}[]{|c|cc|}\hline\cr{\rm dim}&\vrule\lx@intercol\hfil\mbox{Subalgebras}\hfil\lx@intercol\vrule\lx@intercol\\
\hline\cr\hline\cr 4&\mathfrak{gl}_{2}&\\
3&\mathfrak{sl}_{2},&\left\langle\begin{pmatrix}*&0\\
*&*\end{pmatrix}\right\rangle\\
2&\left\langle\begin{pmatrix}*&0\\
0&*\end{pmatrix}\right\rangle,&\left\langle\begin{pmatrix}\lambda_{1}&0\\
*&\lambda_{2}\end{pmatrix}\right\rangle\\
1&\left\langle\begin{pmatrix}\lambda_{1}&0\\
0&\lambda_{2}\end{pmatrix}\right\rangle,&\left\langle\begin{pmatrix}\lambda&0\\
1&\lambda\end{pmatrix}\right\rangle\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

Table 7.
Classification over
{\mathbb{C}}
of subalgebras of
\mathfrak{gl}_{2}
, up to conjugacy

Almost all such subalgebras contain a
semisimple
(diagonalizable) element. Given
S_{0}\in{\mathfrak{s}}_{0}=\mathrm{gr}_{0}({\mathfrak{f}}^{0})
(nonzero) semisimple, we use the
P
-action to normalize the tails of
S=S_{0}+...\in{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
as much as possible. It turns out that this is always possible (Lemma
4.4
), so for purposes of giving an outline let us assume henceforth that
S=S_{0}\in{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
. The existence of such a semisimple element strongly restricts the filtered deformations of
{\mathfrak{s}}
that can arise. Namely, choose an
S
-invariant graded subspace
{\mathfrak{s}}^{\perp}\subset{\mathfrak{g}}
complementary to
{\mathfrak{s}}\subset{\mathfrak{g}}
. Then
{\mathfrak{f}}
is spanned by
x+{\mathfrak{d}}(x)\in{\mathfrak{f}}^{i}
, where
x\in{\mathfrak{s}}_{i}
, with
tail

{\mathfrak{d}}(x)\in\bigoplus_{k>0}({\mathfrak{s}}^{\perp})_{i+k}
, i.e. this unique
deformation map

{\mathfrak{d}}
is of positive homogeneity:

(4.7)

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{d}}\in({\mathfrak{s}}^{*}\otimes{\mathfrak{s}}^{\perp})_{+}.

For
x\in{\mathfrak{s}}_{i}
,
x+{\mathfrak{d}}(x)\in{\mathfrak{f}}^{i}
, and
[S,x+{\mathfrak{d}}(x)]=[S,x]+[S,{\mathfrak{d}}(x)]\in{\mathfrak{f}}^{i}
, where
[S,x]\in{\mathfrak{s}}_{i}
and
[S,{\mathfrak{d}}(x)]\in{\mathfrak{s}}^{\perp}\cap{\mathfrak{g}}^{i+1}
. By uniqueness of
{\mathfrak{d}}
, we have
[S,{\mathfrak{d}}(x)]={\mathfrak{d}}([S,x])
, so
{\mathfrak{d}}
is
S
-annihilated:

(4.8)

\displaystyle S\cdot{\mathfrak{d}}=0.

The two constraints (
4.7
) and (
4.8
) give a priori restrictions on the admissible filtered deformations, which will be efficiently obtained via eigenvalue (weight) considerations.

The cases not containing a semisimple element are the (1-dimensional) Jordan cases
\left\langle\begin{pmatrix}\lambda&0\\
1&\lambda\end{pmatrix}\right\rangle
, so we may assume
{\mathfrak{s}}_{0}=\langle f_{01}+r\mathsf{Z}_{1}\rangle
. The
r\neq 0
yields no new models (beyond those found when assuming existence of a semisimple element), while the
r=0
(i.e. nilpotent) case is the most complicated case, and leads to one more model. Here is a (complex) classification summary:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{|c|c|c|}\hline\cr{\mathfrak{s}}_{0}\mbox{-element}&\mbox{Constraint}&\mbox{Models}\\
\hline\cr\hline\cr\mathsf{Z}_{1}&-&\mathsf{M9}\\
\hline\cr\mathsf{H}&\mathsf{Z}_{1}\not\in{\mathfrak{s}}_{0}&\mathsf{M8},\,\,\mathsf{M6S}\\
\hline\cr\begin{array}[]{c}\mathsf{Z}_{1}+c\mathsf{H}\\
(c\neq 0)\end{array}&{\mathfrak{s}}_{0}\subseteq\langle\mathsf{Z}_{1}+c\mathsf{H},f_{01}\rangle&\mathsf{M7}\mbox{ when $c=1$}\\
\hline\cr f_{01}+r\mathsf{Z}_{1}&{\rm dim}\,{\mathfrak{s}}_{0}=1&\mathsf{M6N}\mbox{ when $r=0$}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

4.4.2.
Semisimple cases

Lemma 4.4
.

Suppose that one of the following hold:

(1)

S_{0}=\mathsf{Z}_{1}\in{\mathfrak{s}}_{0}
;

(2)

\mathsf{Z}_{1}\not\in{\mathfrak{s}}_{0}
and
S_{0}=\mathsf{H}\in{\mathfrak{s}}_{0}
;

(3)

\mathsf{Z}_{1}\not\in{\mathfrak{s}}_{0}
and
S_{0}=\mathsf{Z}_{1}+c\mathsf{H}\in{\mathfrak{s}}_{0}
.

Then, normalizing by the
P
-action, we may assume that
S=S_{0}\in{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
.

Proof.

Let
S_{0}=\mathsf{Z}_{1}+c\mathsf{H}\in{\mathfrak{s}}_{0}
. Then
\{e_{10},e_{11},e_{21},e_{31},e_{32}\}
is an
{\rm ad}_{S_{0}}|_{{\mathfrak{g}}^{1}}
-eigenbasis with eigenvalues

(4.15)

\displaystyle(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2},\lambda_{3},\lambda_{4},\lambda_{5})=(1-c,\quad 1+c,\quad 2,\quad 3-c,\quad 3+c).

Suppose
c\not\in\{\pm 1,\pm 3\}
, so all
\lambda_{i}\neq 0
. Let
S=S_{0}+s_{1}e_{10}+s_{2}e_{11}+s_{3}e_{21}+s_{4}e_{31}+s_{5}e_{32}\in{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
. Given
x\in{\mathfrak{g}}^{1}
, we have
{\rm Ad}_{\exp(x)}(S)=\exp({\rm ad}_{x})(S)=S+[x,S]+\frac{1}{2}[x,[x,S]]+...
Restrict to
x=x_{1}e_{10}+x_{2}e_{11}\in{\mathfrak{g}}_{1}
:

(4.16)

\displaystyle\widetilde{S}={\rm Ad}_{\exp(x)}(S)

\displaystyle=S_{0}+[x,S_{0}]+...=S_{0}+\widetilde{s_{1}}e_{10}+\widetilde{s_{2}}e_{11}+...

where
(\widetilde{s_{1}},\widetilde{s_{2}})=(s_{1}-x_{1}\lambda_{1},s_{2}-x_{2}\lambda_{2})
, and the final ellipsis denotes higher homogeneity terms. Choosing
(x_{1},x_{2})=(\frac{s_{1}}{\lambda_{1}},\frac{s_{2}}{\lambda_{2}})
yields
\widetilde{s_{1}}=\widetilde{s_{2}}=0
for
\widetilde{S}\in\widetilde{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}\subset\widetilde{\mathfrak{f}}
. Dropping tildes, we have
s_{1}=s_{2}=0
. Similarly using
{\mathfrak{g}}_{2}
and
{\mathfrak{g}}_{3}
, we normalize
s_{3}=s_{4}=s_{5}=0
. This handles (1) and most of (3).

Consider the remaining part of (3), i.e.
S_{0}=\mathsf{Z}_{1}+c\mathsf{H}\in{\mathfrak{s}}_{0}
with
c\in\{\pm 1,\pm 3\}
, with the additional hypothesis
\mathsf{Z}_{1}\not\in{\mathfrak{s}}_{0}
. From Table
7
, we may assume (via the action of
G_{0}\subset P
) that
{\mathfrak{s}}_{0}\subseteq\langle\mathsf{Z}_{1}+c\mathsf{H},f_{01}\rangle
. As above, we normalize using
P_{+}
, but there is a residual tail term for
S
(depending on
s
):

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{|c||c|c|c|c|}\hline\cr c&-1&1&-3&3\\
\hline\cr S\in{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}&\mathsf{Z}_{1}-\mathsf{Z}_{2}+se_{11}&\mathsf{Z}_{2}+se_{10}&2\mathsf{Z}_{1}-3\mathsf{Z}_{2}+se_{32}&-\mathsf{Z}_{1}+3\mathsf{Z}_{2}+se_{31}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

In fact,
s=0
is forced in each case. For example when
c=-3
,
\exists X_{5}\in{\mathfrak{f}}^{-3}
with
X_{5}\equiv f_{32}\,{\rm mod}\ {\mathfrak{p}}
. (This is possible since
f_{32}\in{\mathfrak{s}}_{-3}
and tails of
X_{5}
along
f_{21}\in{\mathfrak{s}}_{-2}
and
f_{11},f_{10}\in{\mathfrak{s}}_{-1}
can be eliminated.)
Then
{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}\ni[S,X_{5}]\equiv s[e_{32},f_{32}]\equiv s\mathsf{Z}_{2}\,\,\,{\rm mod}\ \langle f_{01},e_{01}\rangle\oplus{\mathfrak{g}}^{1}
. But
{\mathfrak{s}}_{0}\subset\langle 2\mathsf{Z}_{1}-3\mathsf{Z}_{2},f_{01}\rangle
, so
s=0
. (The other cases are similar, using
[e_{11},f_{11}]=-\mathsf{Z}_{1}+3\mathsf{Z}_{2}
,
[e_{10},f_{10}]=2\mathsf{Z}_{1}-3\mathsf{Z}_{2}
,
[e_{31},f_{31}]=\mathsf{Z}_{1}-\mathsf{Z}_{2}
.)

Finally, assume (2), i.e.
\mathsf{Z}_{1}\not\in{\mathfrak{s}}_{0}
, but
\mathsf{H}\in{\mathfrak{s}}_{0}
. Then
\exists S\in{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
with
S\equiv\mathsf{H}\,{\rm mod}\ {\mathfrak{g}}^{1}
. Since
{\rm ad}_{\mathsf{H}}|_{{\mathfrak{g}}^{1}}
has zero-eigenspace
{\mathfrak{g}}_{2}=\langle e_{21}\rangle
, then we normalize
S=\mathsf{H}+s\,e_{21}\in{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
. We know
\exists X_{3}\in{\mathfrak{f}}^{-2}
with
X_{3}\equiv f_{21}\,{\rm mod}\ {\mathfrak{p}}
, and
{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}\ni[S,X_{3}]\equiv s[e_{21},f_{21}]\equiv s\mathsf{Z}_{1}\,\,\,{\rm mod}\ \langle f_{01},e_{01}\rangle\oplus{\mathfrak{g}}^{1}
, so
s=0
.
∎

Lemma 4.5
.

Suppose
0\neq S:=c_{1}\mathsf{Z}_{1}+c_{2}\mathsf{H}\in{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
. If
c_{2}\neq\pm c_{1}
, then the
{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
-invariant line field
\ell=\langle L\rangle\,{\rm mod}\ {\mathfrak{f}}^{0}\subset{\mathfrak{f}}^{-2}/{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
has the form
L\equiv f_{21}\,{\rm mod}\ {\mathfrak{p}}
.

Proof.

The
{\rm ad}_{S}
-eigenvalues of
f_{21},f_{11},f_{10}
are
-2c_{1},-c_{1}-c_{2},-c_{1}+c_{2}
. By hypothesis, the last two are distinct from the first, so
{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
-invariance of
\ell
implies the result.
∎

4.4.3.
\mathsf{Z}_{1}\in{\mathfrak{s}}_{0}

By Lemma
4.4
, assume
S:=\mathsf{Z}_{1}\in{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
. Take
{\mathfrak{s}}^{\perp}={\mathfrak{g}}_{+}
. then
{\mathfrak{d}}=0
by (
4.7
) and (
4.8
). Thus,
{\mathfrak{f}}=\langle f_{10},f_{11},f_{21},f_{31},f_{32}\rangle\oplus{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
. Note that
f_{21}
spans the
-2
eigenspace of
{\rm ad}_{\mathsf{Z}_{1}}|_{\mathfrak{g}}
, so
\ell=\langle f_{21}\rangle\,{\rm mod}\ {\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
.
Let
U=U_{0}+U_{+}\in{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
, where
U_{0}\in{\mathfrak{g}}_{0}
and
U_{+}\in{\mathfrak{g}}_{+}
. Then
[\mathsf{Z}_{1},U]=[\mathsf{Z}_{1},U_{+}]\in{\mathfrak{f}}^{1}=0
. But
\ker({\rm ad}_{\mathsf{Z}_{1}}|_{{\mathfrak{g}}_{+}})=0
, so
U_{+}=0
. Thus,
{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}:={\mathfrak{g}}_{0}=\mathfrak{gl}_{2}
, which induces
{\mathfrak{f}}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{\mathfrak{f}}
and
\ell\hookrightarrow\widetilde{\ell}:=\langle f_{21}\,{\rm mod}\ \widetilde{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}\rangle
. Since
{\rm dim}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{f}})=9
, we label the tilded structure as
\mathsf{M9}
.

4.4.4.
\mathsf{Z}_{1}\not\in{\mathfrak{s}}_{0}
and
\mathsf{H}\in{\mathfrak{s}}_{0}

From Table
7
, we have
{\mathfrak{s}}_{0}\subseteq\mathfrak{sl}_{2}=\langle f_{01},\mathsf{H},e_{01}\rangle
, and by Lemma
4.4
, we may assume
S:=\mathsf{H}\in{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
. Note
[\mathsf{H},e_{01}]=2e_{01}
and
[\mathsf{H},f_{01}]=-2f_{01}
, while
\pm 2
are not eigenvalues of
{\rm ad}_{\mathsf{H}}|_{{\mathfrak{g}}_{+}}
. By closure under
{\rm ad}_{\mathsf{H}}
, we must have
{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}\subseteq\langle f_{01},\mathsf{H},e_{01}\rangle
.

Choose
{\mathfrak{s}}^{\perp}=\langle f_{01},\mathsf{Z}_{1},e_{01}\rangle\oplus{\mathfrak{g}}_{+}
. Then
{\mathfrak{d}}\in({\mathfrak{s}}^{*}\otimes{\mathfrak{s}}^{\perp})_{+}
satisfies
\mathsf{H}\cdot{\mathfrak{d}}=0
, i.e.
{\mathfrak{d}}
is a sum of weight vectors with weights that are multiples of
2\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}
. For example, take
{\mathfrak{f}}^{-1}\ni X_{1}\equiv f_{10}\,{\rm mod}\ {\mathfrak{p}}
. The tail terms correspond to

(4.19)

\displaystyle f_{10}^{*}\otimes f_{01},\quad f_{10}^{*}\otimes\mathsf{Z}_{1},\quad f_{10}^{*}\otimes\mathsf{Z}_{2},\quad f_{10}^{*}\otimes e_{01},

(4.20)

\displaystyle f_{10}^{*}\otimes e_{10},\quad f_{10}^{*}\otimes e_{11},\quad f_{10}^{*}\otimes e_{21},\quad f_{10}^{*}\otimes e_{31},\quad f_{10}^{*}\otimes e_{32},

but
\mathsf{H}=-\mathsf{Z}_{1}+2\mathsf{Z}_{2}
acts on these with eigenvalues
-3,-1,-1,1
in the first row, and
-2,0,-1,-2,0
in the second row. Since
\mathsf{H}\cdot{\mathfrak{d}}=0
, then only the zero eigenvalue terms are relevant, i.e.
f_{10}^{*}\otimes e_{11}
and
f_{10}^{*}\otimes e_{32}
, with weights
2\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}
and
4\alpha_{1}+2\alpha_{2}
respectively. Consequently,

(4.21)

\displaystyle X_{1}

\displaystyle=f_{10}+t_{1}e_{11}+t_{2}e_{32}\in{\mathfrak{f}}^{-1}.

Doing this similarly for
X_{2}\in{\mathfrak{f}}^{-1}
with
X_{2}\equiv f_{11}\,{\rm mod}\ {\mathfrak{p}}
, we must have

(4.22)

\displaystyle X_{2}

\displaystyle=f_{11}+t_{3}e_{10}+t_{4}e_{31}\in{\mathfrak{f}}^{-1}.

Now use the residual exponential freedom
\exp({\mathfrak{g}}_{2})\subset P_{+}
to normalize: using
\exp(xe_{21})
, we have

(4.23)

\displaystyle(\widetilde{t_{1}},\widetilde{t_{3}})=(t_{1}-2x,t_{3}+2x).

Setting
x=\frac{t_{1}-t_{3}}{4}
, we ensure
\widetilde{t_{1}}=\widetilde{t_{3}}
. Dropping tildes, we may assume that
t_{1}=t_{3}=:a
. Then:

(4.24)

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{f}}^{-2}\ni X_{3}

\displaystyle:=-\frac{1}{2}[X_{1},X_{2}]+\frac{3a}{2}\mathsf{H}\,\,\,=f_{21}+\left(a^{2}+\frac{t_{2}+t_{4}}{2}\right)e_{21},

(4.25)

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{f}}^{-3}\ni X_{4}

\displaystyle:=+\frac{1}{3}[X_{1},X_{3}]-\frac{2a}{3}X_{1}=f_{31}+\frac{2t_{2}+t_{4}}{3}e_{11}+a\left(a^{2}-\frac{t_{2}}{6}+\frac{t_{4}}{2}\right)e_{32},

(4.26)

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{f}}^{-3}\ni X_{5}

\displaystyle:=-\frac{1}{3}[X_{2},X_{3}]-\frac{2a}{3}X_{2}=f_{32}+\frac{t_{2}+2t_{4}}{3}e_{10}+a\left(a^{2}+\frac{t_{2}}{2}-\frac{t_{4}}{6}\right)e_{31}.

Lemma 4.6
.

We have
t_{2}=t_{4}=:b
with
ab=0
, but
(a,b)\neq(0,0)
. Moreover,
\ell=\langle X_{3}\rangle\,{\rm mod}\ {\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
.

Proof.

Note
[X_{1},X_{5}]+aX_{3}-(t_{2}+t_{4})\mathsf{H}=\frac{t_{2}-t_{4}}{3}\mathsf{Z}_{1}-\frac{2a(t_{2}+t_{4})}{3}e_{21}\in{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
. But
\mathsf{Z}_{1}\not\in{\mathfrak{s}}_{0}
by hypothesis, so
t_{2}=t_{4}=:b
and the above lies in
{\mathfrak{f}}^{2}=0
, which forces
ab=0
. Lemma
4.5
and (
4.24
) imply
\ell=\langle X_{3}\rangle\,{\rm mod}\ {\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
.

Assume
a=b=0
. Then
f_{10},f_{11},f_{21},f_{31},f_{32}\in{\mathfrak{f}}
and
\ell=\langle f_{21}\rangle\,{\rm mod}\ {\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
. Let
U=r\mathsf{Z}_{1}+U_{0}+U_{+}\in{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
, where
U_{0}\in\langle f_{01},\mathsf{H},e_{01}\rangle\subset{\mathfrak{g}}_{0}
and
U_{+}\in{\mathfrak{g}}_{+}
. Then
[U,f_{21}]=-2rf_{21}+[U_{+},f_{21}]
. But
{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
-invariance of
\ell
forces
[U_{+},f_{21}]\in{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
, while
\mathrm{im}({\rm ad}_{f_{21}}|_{{\mathfrak{g}}_{+}})=\langle f_{10},f_{11},\mathsf{Z}_{1},e_{10},e_{11}\rangle
, so we must have
[U_{+},f_{21}]=0
(since
\mathsf{Z}_{1}\not\in{\mathfrak{s}}_{0}
and
{\mathfrak{f}}^{1}=0
). Since
\ker({\rm ad}_{f_{21}}|_{{\mathfrak{g}}_{+}})=0
, then
U_{+}=0
. Thus, we have an inclusion into the
\mathsf{M9}
model, which contradicts maximality.
∎

4.4.5.
a=0
,
b\neq 0

Use
\exp(t\mathsf{Z}_{1})
to normalize
1=\widetilde{b}=b\exp(4t)
. Dropping tildes:

(4.27)

\displaystyle X_{1}=f_{10}+e_{32},\quad X_{2}=f_{11}+e_{31},\quad X_{3}=f_{21}+e_{21},\quad X_{4}=f_{31}+e_{11},\quad X_{5}=f_{32}+e_{10}.

Note
[X_{1},X_{4}]=4e_{01}
and
[X_{2},X_{5}]=4f_{01}
, so
e_{01},f_{01}\in{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
. Since
\mathsf{Z}_{1}\not\in{\mathfrak{s}}_{0}
, then
{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}=\langle f_{01},\mathsf{H},e_{01}\rangle
and
{\rm dim}({\mathfrak{f}})=8
. The
{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
-invariant line field is
\ell=\langle X_{3}\rangle\,{\rm mod}\ {\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
. We label this structure
\mathsf{M8}
.

4.4.6.
a\neq 0
,
b=0

Use
\exp(x\mathsf{Z}_{1})
to normalize
1=\widetilde{a}=a\exp(2x)
. Dropping tildes:

(4.28)

\displaystyle X_{1}=f_{10}+e_{11},\quad X_{2}=f_{11}+e_{10},\quad X_{3}=f_{21}+e_{21},\quad X_{4}=f_{31}+e_{32},\quad X_{5}=f_{32}+e_{31}.

We know
\mathsf{H}\in{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
, and
\mathsf{H}
-invariance of the line field forces
\ell=\langle X_{3}\rangle\,{\rm mod}\ {\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
.

Recall that
\langle\mathsf{H}\rangle\subseteq{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}\subseteq\langle f_{01},\mathsf{H},e_{01}\rangle
. We have
{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
-invariant
{\mathcal{D}}={\mathfrak{f}}^{-1}/{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}=\langle X_{1},X_{2}\rangle\,{\rm mod}\ {\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
, but if
U=u_{1}f_{01}+u_{2}e_{01}\in{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
, then
[U,X_{1}]-u_{2}X_{2}=-2u_{2}e_{10}
and
[U,X_{2}]-u_{1}X_{1}=-2u_{1}e_{11}
, which forces
u_{1}=u_{2}=0
since both lie in
{\mathfrak{f}}^{1}=0
. Thus,
{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}=\langle\mathsf{H}\rangle
, and
{\rm dim}({\mathfrak{f}}^{0})=6
. We label this structure
\mathsf{M6S}
.

4.4.7.
Generic case:
\mathsf{Z}_{1}\not\in{\mathfrak{s}}_{0}
,
S_{0}:=\mathsf{Z}_{1}+c\mathsf{H}\in{\mathfrak{s}}_{0}
with
c^{2}\in{\mathbb{C}}\backslash\{0,1,9\}

Via the
G_{0}
-action, we have either
{\mathfrak{s}}_{0}=\langle S_{0},f_{01}\rangle
or
{\mathfrak{s}}_{0}=\langle S_{0}\rangle
, and define
{\mathfrak{s}}^{\perp}=\langle\mathsf{Z}_{1},e_{01}\rangle
or
{\mathfrak{s}}^{\perp}=\langle f_{01},\mathsf{Z}_{1},e_{01}\rangle
respectively.
By Lemma
4.4
, we may assume
S:=\mathsf{Z}_{1}+c\mathsf{H}\in{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
. The zero
S
-eigenvalue terms in
f_{10}^{*}\otimes{\mathfrak{s}}^{\perp}
and
f_{11}^{*}\otimes{\mathfrak{s}}^{\perp}
correspond to the admissible filtered deformations:

(4.29)

\displaystyle X_{1}

\displaystyle=f_{10}+t_{1/3}f_{01}+t_{2}e_{31}\in{\mathfrak{f}}^{-1},

(4.30)

\displaystyle X_{2}

\displaystyle=f_{11}+t_{-1/3}e_{01}+t_{-2}e_{32}\in{\mathfrak{f}}^{-1},

where
t_{i}\in\delta^{c}_{i}{\mathbb{C}}:=\begin{cases}{\mathbb{C}},&c=i;\\
0,&c\neq i.\end{cases}
Since
t_{i}t_{j}=0
for
i\neq j
, then taking brackets yields

(4.31)

\displaystyle X_{3}=f_{21}\in{\mathfrak{f}}^{-2},\quad X_{4}=f_{31}-\frac{t_{2}}{3}e_{10}\in{\mathfrak{f}}^{-3},\quad X_{5}=f_{32}-\frac{t_{-2}}{3}e_{11}\in{\mathfrak{f}}^{-3}.

Furthermore,

(4.32)

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{f}}\ni[X_{1},X_{4}]

\displaystyle=t_{2}\left(\frac{5}{3}\mathsf{Z}_{1}-2\mathsf{Z}_{2}\right)-t_{1/3}f_{32},

(4.33)

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{f}}\ni[X_{2},X_{5}]

\displaystyle=t_{-2}\left(-\frac{1}{3}\mathsf{Z}_{1}+2\mathsf{Z}_{2}\right)-t_{-1/3}f_{31}.

When
c=\pm 2
, these lie in
{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
. We have
{\mathfrak{s}}_{0}\subseteq\langle-\mathsf{Z}_{1}+4\mathsf{Z}_{2},f_{01}\rangle
when
c=2
, while
{\mathfrak{s}}_{0}\subseteq\langle 3\mathsf{Z}_{1}-4\mathsf{Z}_{2},f_{01}\rangle
when
c=-2
. Thus,
t_{2}=t_{-2}=0
. Since
f_{21},f_{11},f_{10}
have distinct
{\rm ad}_{S}
-eigenvalues, then
S
-invariance forces
\ell=\langle X_{3}\rangle\,{\rm mod}\ {\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
.

If
{\rm dim}({\mathfrak{f}}^{0})=1
, there is a clear inclusion into the
\mathsf{M9}
model. If
{\rm dim}({\mathfrak{f}}^{0})=2
, then the zero
S
-eigenvalue terms of
f_{01}^{*}\otimes{\mathfrak{s}}^{\perp}
with positive homogeneity force

(4.34)

\displaystyle N=f_{01}+n_{-1/3}e_{11}\in{\mathfrak{f}}^{0},

where
n_{-1/3}\in\delta^{c}_{-1/3}{\mathbb{C}}
.
But then
[N,X_{1}]-X_{2}=-(t_{-1/3}+3n_{-1/3})e_{01}\in{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
. Since
e_{01}\not\in{\mathfrak{s}}_{0}
, then
t_{-1/3}=-3n_{-1/3}
. Hence,
[N,X_{2}]=n_{-1/3}\left(-4\mathsf{Z}_{1}+9\mathsf{Z}_{2}\right)\in{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
. Since
{\mathfrak{s}}_{0}=\langle\frac{4}{3}\mathsf{Z}_{1}-\frac{2}{3}\mathsf{Z}_{2},f_{01}\rangle
when
c=-\frac{1}{3}
, then
n_{-1/3}=0
. We again embed into
\mathsf{M9}
.

4.4.8.
Non-generic cases

Let
c\in\{\pm 1,\pm 3\}
. By Lemma
4.4
, assume
S:=\mathsf{Z}_{1}+c\mathsf{H}\in{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
(or a multiple thereof).
See Table
8
for a summary.

\begin{array}[]{|@{}c@{}|c|c|c|c|@{}c@{}|c|cc}\hline\cr c&-1&1&-3&3\\
\hline\cr\hline\cr S\in{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}&\mathsf{Z}_{1}-\mathsf{Z}_{2}&\mathsf{Z}_{2}&2\mathsf{Z}_{1}-3\mathsf{Z}_{2}&-\mathsf{Z}_{1}+3\mathsf{Z}_{2}\\
\hline\cr\mbox{Freedom in $P_{+}$}&\exp(\langle e_{11}\rangle)&\exp(\langle e_{10}\rangle)&\exp(\langle e_{32}\rangle)&\exp(\langle e_{31}\rangle)\\
\hline\cr\begin{array}[]{c}N\in{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}\\
{\tiny\mbox{if ${\rm dim}({\mathfrak{f}}^{0})=2$}}\end{array}&f_{01}+n_{1}e_{10}+n_{2}e_{21}+n_{3}e_{32}&f_{01}&f_{01}+ne_{31}&f_{01}\\
\hline\cr X_{1}\in{\mathfrak{f}}^{-1}&f_{10}+t_{1}e_{01}&f_{10}+t_{1}\mathsf{Z}_{1}+t_{2}e_{10}&f_{10}&f_{10}+te_{21}\\
\hline\cr X_{2}\in{\mathfrak{f}}^{-1}&f_{11}+t_{2}\mathsf{Z}_{1}+t_{3}e_{11}&f_{11}+t_{3}f_{01}&f_{11}+te_{21}&f_{11}\\
\hline\cr\begin{array}[]{c}{\tiny\mbox{Line field generator}}\\
X_{3}\in{\mathfrak{f}}^{-2}\end{array}&f_{21}+t_{4}f_{10}+t_{5}e_{01}&f_{21}+t_{4}f_{11}+t_{5}f_{01}&f_{21}-te_{11}&f_{21}-te_{10}\\
\hline\cr{\tiny\begin{tabular}[]{c}Normalization\\
via freedom in $P_{+}$\end{tabular}}&t_{4}=0&t_{4}=0&t=0&t=0\\
\hline\cr\mbox{Maximal?}&\mbox{No }(\mathsf{M9})&\mbox{No }(\mathsf{M7})&\mbox{No }(\mathsf{M9})&\mbox{No }(\mathsf{M9})\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

Table 8.
{\rm dim}({\mathfrak{f}}^{0})=1
cases with
\ell=\langle X_{3}\rangle\,{\rm mod}\ {\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
and
{\mathcal{D}}=\langle X_{1},X_{2}\rangle\,{\rm mod}\ {\mathfrak{f}}^{0}

We proceed by classifying
S
-invariant filtered deformations as above. This yields
S,X_{1},X_{2},X_{3}
(and
N
if
{\rm dim}({\mathfrak{f}}^{0})=2
) as in Table
8
. The residual freedom in
P_{+}
stabilizing the normalization of
S
is used to normalize the generator
X_{3}\,{\rm mod}\ {\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
of the line field
\ell\subset{\mathfrak{f}}^{-2}/{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
. When
c=\pm 3
, the model embeds into
\mathsf{M9}
. (When
c=-3
and
{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}=\langle S,N\rangle
, we observe
n=0
from
[N,X_{3}]=-ne_{10}\in{\mathfrak{f}}^{1}=0
.) So it remains to examine
c=\pm 1
.

Suppose
{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}=\langle S\rangle
. There exists a
G_{0}
element
\sigma
that induces
(\mathsf{Z}_{1},\mathsf{H})\mapsto(\mathsf{Z}_{1},-\mathsf{H})
, which induces
c\mapsto-c
. (In terms of
2\times 2
matrices,
\sigma=\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
1&0\end{pmatrix}
, and
\sigma\operatorname{diag}(1+c,1-c)\sigma^{-1}=\operatorname{diag}(1-c,1+c)
.) Thus, it suffices to examine
c=1
. In this case, the model embeds into
\mathsf{M7}
by enlarging
{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
by
f_{01}
.

Suppose
{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}=\langle S,N\rangle
.

•

c=1
: we can WLOG take
t_{3}=t_{5}=0
by adding multiples of
N
. Necessarily
t_{2}\neq 0
, otherwise the model embeds into
\mathsf{M9}
. Applying a rescaling by
\exp(\langle\mathsf{Z}_{1}\rangle)\subset G_{0}
, we get
\langle X_{1}\rangle\mapsto\langle\lambda^{-1}f_{10}+t_{1}\mathsf{Z}_{1}+\lambda t_{2}e_{10}\rangle=\langle f_{10}+\widetilde{t}_{1}\mathsf{Z}_{1}+\widetilde{t}_{2}e_{10}\rangle
, where
(\widetilde{t}_{1},\widetilde{t}_{2})=(\lambda t_{1},\lambda^{2}t_{2})
. Over
{\mathbb{C}}
, we normalize
t_{2}=1
, and write
t_{1}=:\mathsf{a}
. We label these structures
\mathsf{M7_{a}}
.

•

c=-1
: take
S,N,X_{1},X_{2},X_{3}
as in Table
8
. Then

(4.35)

\displaystyle[N,X_{3}]-2t_{5}S=-2n_{1}f_{11}+(n_{2}-t_{5})\mathsf{Z}_{1}+(n_{1}t_{5}+n_{3})e_{11}\in{\mathfrak{f}}^{-1}.

Since
\ell=\langle X_{3}\rangle\,{\rm mod}\ {\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
is
{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
-invariant, then
n_{1}=0
. But then
[N,X_{3}]\in{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
and
{\mathfrak{f}}^{1}=0
forces

n_{2}=t_{5}
and
n_{3}=0
. Furthermore,

(4.36)

\displaystyle[N,X_{1}]-X_{2}-2t_{1}S=-(t_{1}+t_{2})\mathsf{Z}_{1}-(t_{3}+2t_{5})e_{11}\in{\mathfrak{f}}^{0},

so
t_{2}=-t_{1}
and
t_{3}=-2t_{5}
. Moreover,
[N,X_{2}]=4t_{5}e_{10}+2t_{1}t_{5}e_{21}+6t_{5}^{2}e_{32}\in{\mathfrak{f}}^{1}
, so
t_{5}=0
is forced. This yields
(S,N,X_{1},X_{2},X_{3},X_{4},X_{5})=(\mathsf{Z}_{1}-\mathsf{Z}_{2},f_{01},f_{10}+t_{1}e_{01},f_{11}-t_{1}\mathsf{Z}_{1},f_{21},f_{31},f_{32})
, which embeds into
\mathsf{M9}
.

We have completed our study of all
{\mathfrak{s}}_{0}
that contains a semisimple element. From Table
7
, it remains to consider
{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}=\langle N\rangle
with
N_{0}=\mathrm{gr}_{0}(N)=f_{01}+r\mathsf{Z}_{1}\in{\mathfrak{s}}_{0}
.

4.4.9.
Jordan non-nilpotent case

Let
{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}=\langle N\rangle
with
N_{0}=\mathrm{gr}_{0}(N)=f_{01}+r\mathsf{Z}_{1}\in{\mathfrak{s}}_{0}
.
Since
r\neq 0
, then
{\rm ad}_{N_{0}}|_{{\mathfrak{g}}_{+}}
is injective, since it maps

(4.37)

\displaystyle(e_{10},e_{11},e_{21},e_{31},e_{32})\mapsto(re_{10},re_{11}-e_{10},2re_{21},3re_{31},3re_{32}+e_{31}).

Normalizing via the
P_{+}
-action, we may assume
N=N_{0}\in{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
. Let
{\mathfrak{f}}^{-1}\ni X_{2}\equiv f_{11}\,{\rm mod}\ {\mathfrak{p}}
. Since
\mathrm{gr}_{-1}([N,X_{2}])=-rf_{11}
, then
[N,X_{2}]+rX_{2}\in{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}=\langle N\rangle
. Evaluation yields
X_{2}=f_{11}\,{\rm mod}\ {\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
, so WLOG
X_{2}=f_{11}
. Similarly, take
{\mathfrak{f}}^{-1}\ni X_{1}\equiv f_{10}\,{\rm mod}\ {\mathfrak{p}}
. We find that
[S,X_{1}]+rX_{1}-X_{2}\in{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}=\langle N\rangle
. This forces
X_{1}\equiv f_{10}\,{\rm mod}\ {\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
, so WLOG
X_{1}=f_{10}
. Taking brackets, we find
{\mathfrak{f}}^{-2}=\langle f_{21},f_{11},f_{10},N\rangle
. The only
N
-invariant line field is
\ell=\langle X_{3}\rangle\,{\rm mod}\ {\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
with
X_{3}=f_{21}\in{\mathfrak{f}}^{-2}
. This model embeds into
\mathsf{M9}
.

4.4.10.
Nilpotent case

Let
{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}=\langle N\rangle
with
N_{0}=\mathrm{gr}_{0}(N)=f_{01}\in{\mathfrak{s}}_{0}
.

Lemma 4.7
.

Let
N_{0}=f_{01}\in{\mathfrak{s}}_{0}
. Normalizing by the
P
-action, we may assume that

(4.38)

\displaystyle N=N_{0}\in{\mathfrak{f}}^{0},\quad\ell=\langle L\rangle\,{\rm mod}\ {\mathfrak{f}}^{0},

where, for some
a\in{\mathbb{C}}
, we have

(4.39)

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{f}}^{-2}\ni L\equiv f_{21}+af_{11}\quad\,{\rm mod}\ {\mathfrak{p}}.

The element
\exp(x_{1}e_{10}+x_{2}e_{21}+x_{3}e_{31})\in P_{+}
preserving
N=N_{0}
acts via
\widetilde{a}=a-2x_{1}
.

Proof.

Write
{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}\ni N=f_{01}+n_{1}e_{10}+n_{2}e_{11}+n_{3}e_{21}+n_{4}e_{31}+n_{5}e_{32}
. Since
[e_{11},N_{0}]=e_{10}
and
[e_{32},N_{0}]=-e_{31}
, then normalize
n_{1}=n_{4}=0
by exponentiating along
\langle e_{11},e_{32}\rangle
.

For the generator of the line field, write
{\mathfrak{f}}^{-2}\ni L\equiv f_{21}+a_{1}f_{11}+a_{2}f_{10}\,{\rm mod}\ {\mathfrak{p}}
.
Modulo
{\mathfrak{p}}
, we have
[N,L]\equiv 2n_{2}f_{10}+a_{2}f_{11}
, so by
N
-invariance of
\ell
, we must have
n_{2}=a_{2}=0
. Since
[e_{21},f_{21}]=\mathsf{Z}_{1}
and
[e_{32},f_{21}]=e_{11}
, then evaluation again yields

(4.40)

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}\ni[N,L]\equiv n_{3}\mathsf{Z}_{1}+n_{5}e_{11}\quad\,{\rm mod}\ \langle f_{01},\mathsf{H},e_{01},e_{10},e_{21},e_{31},e_{32}\rangle,

so
n_{3}=n_{5}=0
. The final statement follows since
[e_{10},f_{21}]=-2f_{11}
.
∎

So, assume
N=f_{01}
. Next, find generators for
{\mathfrak{f}}
and normalize. Take

(4.41)

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{f}}^{-1}\ni X_{1}

\displaystyle=f_{10}+t_{0}f_{01}+t_{1}\mathsf{Z}_{1}+t_{2}\mathsf{H}+t_{3}e_{01}+t_{4}e_{10}+t_{5}e_{11}+t_{6}e_{21}+t_{7}e_{31}+t_{8}e_{32}.

Adding multiples of
N
, we may assume
t_{0}=0
. Since

(4.42)

\displaystyle[e_{10},f_{10}]=2\mathsf{Z}_{1}-3\mathsf{Z}_{2},\quad[e_{21},f_{10}]=-2e_{11},\quad[e_{31},f_{10}]=e_{21},

and
[e_{10},N]=[e_{21},N]=[e_{31},N]=0
, then normalize
t_{1}=t_{5}=t_{6}=0
by exponentiating along
\langle e_{10},e_{21},e_{31}\rangle
. Define
X_{2}:=[N,X_{1}]-2t_{2}N\in{\mathfrak{f}}^{-1}
, so

(4.43)

\displaystyle X_{1}

\displaystyle=f_{10}+t_{2}\mathsf{H}+t_{3}e_{01}+t_{4}e_{10}+t_{7}e_{31}+t_{8}e_{32},

(4.44)

\displaystyle X_{2}

\displaystyle=f_{11}-t_{3}\mathsf{H}+t_{8}e_{31}.

Next, we iteratively take brackets, and add appropriate linear combinations of higher homogeneity terms to produce
X_{3},X_{4},X_{5}\in{\mathfrak{f}}
with
(X_{3},X_{4},X_{5})\equiv(f_{21},f_{31},f_{32})
, each modulo
{\mathfrak{p}}
. Namely,

(4.45)

\displaystyle X_{3}

\displaystyle:=-\frac{1}{2}[X_{1},X_{2}]-\frac{t_{2}}{2}X_{2}+t_{3}X_{1}-\frac{3}{2}t_{4}N\in{\mathfrak{f}}^{-2}

\displaystyle=f_{21}+\frac{3}{2}t_{2}t_{3}\mathsf{H}+\frac{3}{2}t_{3}t_{4}e_{10}+t_{8}e_{21}+\frac{3}{2}t_{3}t_{7}e_{31}

(4.46)

\displaystyle X_{4}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{3}[X_{1},X_{3}]+\frac{2}{3}t_{4}X_{2}+\frac{t_{2}t_{3}}{2}X_{1}\in{\mathfrak{f}}^{-3}

\displaystyle=f_{31}-t_{3}\left(\frac{t_{2}^{2}}{2}+\frac{t_{4}}{3}\right)\mathsf{Z}_{1}+t_{3}\left(t_{2}^{2}+\frac{t_{4}}{6}\right)\mathsf{Z}_{2}-\frac{t_{2}t_{3}^{2}}{2}e_{01}+\left(\frac{t_{2}t_{3}t_{4}}{2}-\frac{t_{7}}{3}\right)e_{10}

\displaystyle\qquad+\left(t_{8}-\frac{t_{3}^{2}t_{4}}{2}\right)e_{11}-\frac{t_{3}t_{7}}{2}e_{21}+\left(\frac{t_{2}t_{3}t_{7}}{2}-\frac{t_{4}t_{8}}{3}\right)e_{31}+\frac{t_{3}^{2}t_{7}}{2}e_{32}

(4.47)

\displaystyle X_{5}

\displaystyle:=-\frac{1}{3}[X_{2},X_{3}]+\frac{t_{2}t_{3}}{2}X_{2}+\frac{3}{2}t_{3}t_{4}N\in{\mathfrak{f}}^{-3}

\displaystyle=f_{32}-\frac{t_{2}t_{3}^{2}}{2}\mathsf{H}+\left(t_{8}-\frac{t_{3}^{2}t_{4}}{2}\right)e_{10}-\frac{t_{3}^{2}t_{7}}{2}e_{31}

Thus,
{\mathfrak{f}}=\langle N,X_{1},X_{2},X_{3},X_{4},X_{5}\rangle
, but there are further constraints on the parameters.

Lemma 4.8
.

We must have
t_{4}=t_{7}=0
and
t_{8}=\frac{(t_{2}t_{3})^{2}}{4}\neq 0
.

Proof.

Let us evaluate the closure condition
[X_{1},X_{4}]\in{\mathfrak{f}}
. Modulo
\langle e_{01}\rangle\oplus{\mathfrak{g}}_{+}
, we find:

(4.48)

\displaystyle[X_{1},X_{4}]-t_{2}X_{4}-t_{4}X_{3}+t_{3}\left(\frac{t_{2}^{2}}{2}+\frac{t_{4}}{3}\right)X_{1}

\displaystyle\equiv\left(\frac{5t_{7}}{3}+\frac{t_{2}t_{3}t_{4}}{2}\right)\mathsf{Z}_{1}-\left(t_{2}t_{3}t_{4}+2t_{7}\right)\mathsf{Z}_{2}.

Since
[X_{1},X_{4}]\in{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}=\langle N=f_{01}\rangle
, then
[X_{1},X_{4}]=0
, so
t_{7}=0=t_{2}t_{3}t_{4}
. Given this, we have

(4.49)

\displaystyle\begin{split}[X_{1},X_{4}]-t_{2}X_{4}-t_{4}X_{3}+t_{3}\left(\frac{t_{2}^{2}}{2}+\frac{t_{4}}{3}\right)X_{1}&\equiv\left(4t_{8}-(t_{2}t_{3})^{2}-\frac{4}{3}t_{3}^{2}t_{4}\right)e_{01}-\frac{5}{6}t_{3}t_{4}^{2}e_{10}\\
&\qquad\quad\,{\rm mod}\ \langle e_{11},e_{21},e_{31},e_{32}\rangle,\end{split}

so
t_{3}t_{4}=0
and
t_{8}=\frac{(t_{2}t_{3})^{2}}{4}
.

If
t_{3}\neq 0
, then
t_{4}=0
follows from
t_{3}t_{4}=0
. Assume
t_{3}=0
. Then
t_{8}=0
as well, and

(4.50)

\displaystyle(N,X_{1},X_{2},X_{3},X_{4},X_{5})=(f_{01},f_{10}+t_{2}\mathsf{H}+t_{4}e_{10},f_{11},f_{21},f_{31},f_{32}).

From Lemma
4.7
, we know
\ell=\langle L\rangle\,{\rm mod}\ {\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
, where
L\equiv f_{21}+af_{11}\,{\rm mod}\ {\mathfrak{p}}
. Apply
p=\exp(xe_{10})
so that the line field is
\widetilde{\ell}=\langle f_{21}\,{\rm mod}\ \widetilde{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}\rangle
. Then
\widetilde{\mathfrak{f}}={\rm Ad}_{p}{\mathfrak{f}}
is spanned by

(4.51)

\displaystyle(\widetilde{N},\widetilde{X}_{1},\widetilde{X}_{2},\widetilde{X}_{3},\widetilde{X}_{4},\widetilde{X}_{5})=(f_{01},f_{10}+\widetilde{u}\mathsf{Z}_{1}+\widetilde{v}\mathsf{H}+\widetilde{w}e_{10},f_{11},f_{21},f_{31},f_{32}),

where
(\widetilde{u},\widetilde{v},\widetilde{w})=(\frac{x}{2},t_{2}-\frac{3}{2}x,t_{4}+xt_{2}-x^{2})
. However, maximality is contradicted from:

•

\widetilde{w}=0
: the model embeds into
\mathsf{M9}
.

•

\widetilde{w}\neq 0
: use
\exp(\langle\mathsf{Z}_{1}\rangle)
to normalize
\widetilde{w}=1
, and then embed into
\mathsf{M7}
.

Next, assume
t_{2}=0
. Then
{\mathfrak{f}}
is spanned by
N=f_{01}
and

(4.52)

\displaystyle X_{1}=f_{10}+t_{3}e_{01},\quad X_{2}=f_{11}-t_{3}\mathsf{H},\quad X_{3}=f_{21},\quad X_{4}=f_{31},\quad X_{5}=f_{32}.

For
\ell=\langle L\rangle\,{\rm mod}\ {\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
, where
L=X_{3}+aX_{2}
, forcing
{\mathcal{T}}\equiv 0
(via (
A.9
)) for the XXO-structure
({\mathfrak{f}},{\mathfrak{f}}^{0};\ell,{\mathcal{D}})
yields
a=0
. There is then an inclusion into the
\mathsf{M9}
model. Thus,
t_{2}\neq 0
.
∎

Since
t_{2}t_{3}\neq 0
, then we normalize both using a
G_{0}
-rescaling. From (
4.43
) and (
4.44
), we find that
\exp(\langle\mathsf{Z}_{1}\rangle)
and
\exp(\langle\mathsf{Z}_{2}\rangle)
yield
(t_{2},t_{3})\mapsto(t_{2}\lambda,t_{3}\lambda\mu)
, where
\lambda,\mu\in{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
. Let us normalize
(t_{2},t_{3})=(2,1)
. Thus,
{\mathfrak{f}}
is spanned by
N=f_{01}
, and

(4.53)

\displaystyle X_{1}

\displaystyle=f_{10}+2\mathsf{H}+e_{01}+e_{32},\quad X_{2}=f_{11}-\mathsf{H}+e_{31},\quad X_{3}=f_{21}+3\mathsf{H}+e_{21},

\displaystyle X_{4}

\displaystyle=f_{31}+2\mathsf{H}-e_{01}+e_{11},\quad X_{5}=f_{32}-\mathsf{H}+e_{10}.

Any
{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
-invariant line field
\ell=\langle L\rangle\,{\rm mod}\ {\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
has generator of the form

(4.54)

\displaystyle L=X_{3}+aX_{2},\quad a\in{\mathbb{C}}.

However, using (
A.9
) and forcing
{\mathcal{T}}\equiv 0
for the XXO-structure
({\mathfrak{f}},{\mathfrak{f}}^{0};\ell,{\mathcal{D}})
implies

(4.55)

\displaystyle a(a-3)(a-4)=0.

When
a=0
, there is an obvious embedding into the
\mathsf{M8}
model. When
a=4
, let
x=2e_{10}+e_{21}+e_{31}
and
\widetilde{\mathfrak{f}}={\rm Ad}_{\exp(x)}{\mathfrak{f}}
, which contains

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{l}\widetilde{X}_{1}:=f_{10}+2(\mathsf{Z}_{1}-\mathsf{Z}_{2})+e_{01}\in\widetilde{\mathfrak{f}}^{-1},\\
\widetilde{X}_{2}:=f_{11}+\mathsf{Z}_{1}-2\mathsf{Z}_{2}\in\widetilde{\mathfrak{f}}^{-1},\end{array}\quad\begin{array}[]{l}\widetilde{N}:=f_{01}\in\widetilde{\mathfrak{f}}^{0},\\
\widetilde{L}:=f_{21}+2(\mathsf{Z}_{1}-\mathsf{Z}_{2})\in\widetilde{\mathfrak{f}}^{-2},\end{array}

where
\widetilde{\ell}=\langle\widetilde{L}\rangle\,{\rm mod}\ \widetilde{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
, and
\widetilde{X}_{4},\widetilde{X}_{5}
are induced via brackets. Clearly, the model embeds into
\mathsf{M9}
.

Proposition 4.1
.

The
\mathsf{M6N}
model (
a=3
) does not embed into a model in Table
6
of larger dimension (or a
P
-translate thereof).

Proof.

When
a=3
, we have
(N,L)=(f_{01},f_{21}+3f_{11}+e_{21}+3e_{31})
. Normalize this via the
P_{+}
-action. Letting
x=x_{1}e_{10}+x_{2}e_{11}+x_{3}e_{21}+x_{4}e_{31}+x_{5}e_{32}\in{\mathfrak{p}}_{+}
, and
\widetilde{\mathfrak{f}}={\rm Ad}_{\exp(x)}{\mathfrak{f}}
, the condition
\widetilde{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}\subset{\mathfrak{g}}_{0}
forces
x_{2}=x_{5}=0
, and the condition
\widetilde{L}\equiv f_{21}\,{\rm mod}\ {\mathfrak{p}}
then forces
x_{1}=\frac{3}{2}
. Then,
\widetilde{N}=f_{01}
and

(4.60)

\displaystyle\widetilde{L}\equiv f_{21}+x_{3}\mathsf{Z}_{1}+\left(\frac{9}{4}x_{3}-x_{4}\right)e_{10}+(1-x_{3}^{2})e_{21}+\left(3x_{3}\left(\frac{9}{4}x_{3}-x_{4}\right)-\frac{3}{2}\right)e_{31}\quad\,{\rm mod}\ \widetilde{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}.

It is clear that the
e_{10}
and
e_{31}
coefficients cannot both be removed via the
P_{+}
-action, nor any further
G_{0}
-action.
From Table
6
, all models of greater dimension are presented with line field generators of the form
L\equiv f_{21}\,{\rm mod}\ {\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
or
L\equiv f_{21}+e_{21}\,{\rm mod}\ {\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
, so the conclusion follows.
∎

4.5.
Real forms

Given the classification of complex admissible
({\mathfrak{f}},{\mathfrak{f}}^{0};\ell,{\mathcal{D}})
from Section
4.4
, we now give the abstract classification of real forms. Recall that an anti-involution of
{\mathfrak{f}}
is a conjugate-linear, bracket-preserving map
\varphi:{\mathfrak{f}}\to{\mathfrak{f}}
such that
\varphi^{2}=\mathrm{id}_{\mathfrak{f}}
.

Definition 4.9
.

For a complex admissible model
({\mathfrak{f}},{\mathfrak{f}}^{0};\ell,{\mathcal{D}})
,

(1)

an
automorphism
is a map
T\in\operatorname{Aut}({\mathfrak{f}})
such that
T({\mathfrak{f}}^{i})={\mathfrak{f}}^{i}
,
\forall i
, and which induces maps that preserve
\ell
and
{\mathcal{D}}
;

(2)

a
real form
is an anti-involution
\varphi:{\mathfrak{f}}\to{\mathfrak{f}}
such that
\varphi({\mathfrak{f}}^{i})={\mathfrak{f}}^{i}
,
\forall i
, and which induces maps that preserve
\ell
and
{\mathcal{D}}
.

Two real forms
\varphi,\widetilde{\varphi}
are
equivalent
if there exists an automorphism
T
such that
\widetilde{\varphi}=T\circ\varphi\circ T^{-1}
.

Each anti-involution
\varphi
yields a real admissible model
({\mathfrak{f}}_{\mathbb{R}},{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}_{\mathbb{R}};\ell_{\mathbb{R}},{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbb{R}})
determined by the (real) fixed point sets, e.g.
{\mathfrak{f}}_{\mathbb{R}}:={\mathfrak{f}}^{\varphi}=\{x\in{\mathfrak{f}}:\varphi(x)=x\}
.

Example 4.10
.

For the
\mathsf{M8.2}
model, the anti-involution
\varphi
given in Table
10
yields

(4.61)

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{f}}_{\mathbb{R}}:={\mathfrak{f}}^{\varphi}=\langle(1+i)X_{1},(1+i)X_{2},iX_{3},(1-i)X_{4},(1-i)X_{5},\mathsf{H},e_{01},f_{01}\rangle.

For the
\mathsf{M6S.3}
model, the anti-involution
\varphi
given in Table
10
yields

(4.62)

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{f}}_{\mathbb{R}}:={\mathfrak{f}}^{\varphi}=\langle X_{1}-X_{2},i(X_{1}+X_{2}),iX_{3},X_{4}-X_{5},i(X_{4}+X_{5}),i\mathsf{H}\rangle.

Our main result is the classification of real forms up to equivalence:

Theorem 4.11
.

The complete classification of real forms of complex admissible models
({\mathfrak{f}},{\mathfrak{f}}^{0};\ell,{\mathcal{D}})
is given in Tables
9
and
10
.

\begin{array}[]{|c|c|c|c|c|c|}\hline\cr\mbox{${\mathbb{C}}$-model}&\mbox{Restrictions}&\mbox{Real forms}&\mbox{Real labels}&\mbox{Symmetry algebra ${\mathfrak{f}}\subset G_{2}$}\\
\hline\cr\hline\cr\mathsf{M9}&-&1&\mathsf{M9}&{\mathfrak{p}}_{1}\\
\hline\cr\hbox{\multirowsetup$\mathsf{M8}$}&\hbox{\multirowsetup$-$}&\hbox{\multirowsetup$2$}&\mathsf{M8.1}&\mathfrak{sl}(3,{\mathbb{R}})\\
&&&\mathsf{M8.2}&\mathfrak{su}(1,2)\\
\hline\cr\hbox{\multirowsetup$\mathsf{M7}_{\mathsf{a}}$}&\mathsf{a}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{\times}\cup i{\mathbb{R}}^{\times}&1&\mathsf{M7}_{\mathsf{a}}&\hbox{\multirowsetup${\mathbb{R}}^{2}\ltimes\mathfrak{heis}_{5}$}\\
&\mathsf{a}=0&2&\mathsf{M7}_{0}^{\pm}&\\
\hline\cr\hbox{\multirowsetup$\mathsf{M6S}$}&\hbox{\multirowsetup$-$}&\hbox{\multirowsetup$3$}&\mathsf{M6S}.1&\mathfrak{sl}(2,{\mathbb{R}})\times\mathfrak{sl}(2,{\mathbb{R}})\\
&&&\mathsf{M6S}.2&\mathfrak{sl}(2,{\mathbb{R}})\times\mathfrak{sl}(2,{\mathbb{R}})\\
&&&\mathsf{M6S}.3&\mathfrak{so}(3)\times\mathfrak{so}(3)\\
\hline\cr\mathsf{M6N}&-&1&\mathsf{M6N}&\mathfrak{aff}(2,{\mathbb{R}})\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

\hline\cr\hline\cr\mathsf{M9}&\begin{array}[]{c}X_{1},X_{2},X_{3},X_{4},X_{5},\\
\mathsf{H},e_{01},f_{01},\mathsf{Z}_{1}\end{array}&\mathsf{M9}&\mathrm{id}\\
\hline\cr\hbox{\multirowsetup$\mathsf{M8}$}&\hbox{\multirowsetup$\begin{array}[]{c}X_{1},X_{2},X_{3},X_{4},X_{5},\\

&&\mathsf{M8.2}&\operatorname{diag}(i,i,-1,-i,-i,1,1,1)\\
\hline\cr\mathsf{M7}_{\mathsf{a}}&\begin{array}[]{c}X_{1},X_{2},X_{3},X_{4},X_{5},\\
\mathsf{Z}_{2},f_{01}\end{array}&\begin{array}[]{l}\mathsf{M7}_{\mathsf{a}\neq 0}\\
\mathsf{M7}_{\mathsf{a}=0}^{\pm}\end{array}&\begin{cases}\mathrm{id},&\mathsf{a}\in{\mathbb{R}}\\
\operatorname{diag}(-1,-1,1,-1,-1,1,1),&\mathsf{a}\in i{\mathbb{R}}\end{cases}\\
\hline\cr\mathsf{M6S}.1&&\mathsf{M6S}.1&\mathrm{id}\\

1&0\end{pmatrix},-1\right)\\
\mathsf{M6S}.3&&\mathsf{M6S}.3&\operatorname{diag}\left(\begin{pmatrix}0&-1\\
-1&0\end{pmatrix},-1,\begin{pmatrix}0&-1\\
-1&0\end{pmatrix},-1\right)\\
\hline\cr\mathsf{M6N}&\begin{array}[]{c}X_{1},X_{2},X_{3},X_{4},X_{5},\\
f_{01}\end{array}&\mathsf{M6N}&\mathrm{id}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

Table 10.
Anti-involutions of complex admissible models
({\mathfrak{f}},{\mathfrak{f}}^{0};\ell,{\mathcal{D}})

The proof of Theorem
4.11
is a straightforward, but tedious computation, and do not provide details here. We take advantage of the fact that any anti-involution
\varphi
of
({\mathfrak{f}},{\mathfrak{f}}^{0};\ell,{\mathcal{D}})
is bracket-preserving, so ideals, derived & lower central series are preserved by
\varphi
, in addition to being filtration and structure preserving. We also note that a source of automorphisms generating equivalences of anti-involutions is
\exp({\mathfrak{f}}^{0})\subset\text{Aut}({\mathfrak{f}})
, so this can be used for some initial normalization of
\varphi
.

5.
Conformal structures

For each (real or complex) admissible XXO-structure
({\mathfrak{f}},{\mathfrak{f}}^{0};\ell,{\mathcal{D}})
in our classification (Table
6
), quotienting by
\ell
yields a well-defined
{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
-invariant conformal structure on
{\mathfrak{f}}/{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
. Integration yields a local coordinate model for a conformal structure whose conformal symmetry algebra acts multiply-transitively, is isomorphic to
{\mathfrak{f}}
, and has isotropy subalgebra isomorphic to
{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
at a generic point.

In this section, for each of these conformal structures, we:

(1)

establish the Petrov classification of the (SD & ASD parts of the) Weyl tensor;

(2)

give a local coordinate model;

(3)

describe the model Cartan-theoretically;

(4)

calculate the conformal holonomy;

(5)

assess the existence of an Einstein representative.

The Petrov classification is confirmed in three independent manners: Lie-theoretically, in a coordinate XXO-fashion, and Cartan-theoretically. A fourth (more traditional) method (which we do not discuss) is to directly compute the Weyl tensor of
[\mathsf{g}]
, decompose it into SD & ASD parts, and write each in a spinorial fashion so that their descriptions as binary quartics manifest themselves.

5.1.
Petrov types

The fundamental tensor for 4D split-conformal structures is the Weyl tensor, which decomposes into SD and ASD parts. Each can be viewed as a binary quartic, so each has a
Petrov type
corresponding to the root type multiplicities:
\mathsf{O}
(quartic vanishes),
\mathsf{N}
(quadruple root),
\mathsf{D}
(two double roots),
\mathsf{III}
(one multiplicity 3 root, one simple root),
\mathsf{II}
(one double root, two other simple roots),
\mathsf{I}
(four distinct simple roots). In the real case, one has further refinements, e.g.
\mathsf{D}^{+}
(two real double roots) and
\mathsf{D}^{-}
(two complex conjugate double roots). We will write the combined Petrov type as
A.B
, where
A
and
B
refer to the SD and ASD Petrov types, respectively.

Theorem 5.1
.

The Petrov types for the conformal structures arising from Table
6
are given below.

\begin{array}[]{|c|c|c|c|c|}\hline\cr{\mathbb{C}}\mbox{-model}&\mbox{Petrov type}&\mbox{Remarks on real forms}\\
\hline\cr\hline\cr\mathsf{M9}&\mathsf{N}.\mathsf{O}&\\
\mathsf{M8}&\mathsf{D}.\mathsf{O}&\begin{cases}\mathsf{M8.1}:\mathsf{D}^{+}.\mathsf{O},\,\,\,{\mathfrak{f}}\cong\mathfrak{sl}(3,{\mathbb{R}})\\
\mathsf{M8.2}:\mathsf{D}^{-}.\mathsf{O},\,\,\,{\mathfrak{f}}\cong\mathfrak{su}(1,2)\end{cases}\\
\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\mathsf{M7}_{\mathsf{a}}\\
\mbox{\tiny$(\mathsf{a}\in{\mathbb{C}})$}\end{array}&\begin{cases}\mathsf{N}.\mathsf{N},&\mathsf{a}^{2}\neq\frac{4}{3}\\
\mathsf{N}.\mathsf{O},&\mathsf{a}^{2}=\frac{4}{3}\end{cases}&\begin{tabular}[]{c}One real form $\mathsf{M7_{a}}$ exists when $\mathsf{a}^{2}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{\times}$;\\
two real forms $\mathsf{M7_{0}^{\pm}}$ exist when $\mathsf{a}=0$.\end{tabular}\\
\mathsf{M6S}&\mathsf{D}.\mathsf{D}&\begin{cases}\mathsf{M6S}.1:\mathsf{D}^{+}.\mathsf{D}^{+},\,\,\,{\mathfrak{f}}\cong\mathfrak{sl}(2,{\mathbb{R}})\times\mathfrak{sl}(2,{\mathbb{R}})\\
\mathsf{M6S}.2:\mathsf{D}^{-}.\mathsf{D}^{-},\,\,\,{\mathfrak{f}}\cong\mathfrak{sl}(2,{\mathbb{R}})\times\mathfrak{sl}(2,{\mathbb{R}})\\
\mathsf{M6S}.3:\mathsf{D}^{-}.\mathsf{D}^{-},\,\,\,{\mathfrak{f}}\cong\mathfrak{so}(3)\times\mathfrak{so}(3)\end{cases}\\
\mathsf{M6N}&\mathsf{III}.\mathsf{O}&\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

We now indicate how Theorem
5.1
was obtained from Lie-theoretic data in Table
6
. For each model there,
\ell=\langle L\rangle\,{\rm mod}\ {\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
and
{\mathcal{D}}=\langle X_{1},X_{2}\rangle\,{\rm mod}\ {\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
. Define
{\mathfrak{k}}:=\langle L\rangle+{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
, so that
{\mathfrak{f}}/{\mathfrak{k}}
is 4-dimensional, and carries a
{\mathfrak{k}}
-invariant conformal structure
[\mathsf{g}]
, where
\mathsf{g}\in S^{2}({\mathfrak{f}}/{\mathfrak{k}})^{*}
. Recall that
{\mathfrak{k}}
-invariance is expressed in matrix form via
A^{\top}\mathsf{g}+\mathsf{g}A=\lambda(A)\mathsf{g},\,\,\forall A\in{\mathfrak{k}}
. We easily confirm:

Lemma 5.2
.

Define
\widetilde{e}_{i}\in{\mathfrak{f}}
below, and define a basis of
{\mathfrak{f}}/{\mathfrak{k}}
given by
e_{i}:=\widetilde{e}_{i}\,{\rm mod}\ {\mathfrak{k}}
.

\begin{array}[]{|c|cccc|c|}\hline\cr\mbox{Model}&\widetilde{e}_{1}&\widetilde{e}_{2}&\widetilde{e}_{3}&\widetilde{e}_{4}\\
\hline\cr\hline\cr\mathsf{M9}&X_{1}&X_{5}&X_{4}&X_{2}\\
\mathsf{M8}&X_{1}&X_{5}&X_{4}&X_{2}\\
\mathsf{M7}_{\mathsf{a}}&X_{1}&X_{5}&X_{4}-\frac{1}{3}X_{2}&X_{2}\\
\mathsf{M6S}&X_{1}&X_{5}+\frac{2}{3}X_{2}&X_{4}&X_{2}\\
\mathsf{M6N}&X_{1}&X_{5}&X_{4}+2X_{2}&X_{2}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

Letting
\{\theta^{i}\}
be the dual basis to
\{e_{i}\}
, the unique
{\mathfrak{k}}
-invariant
[\mathsf{g}]
on
{\mathfrak{f}}/{\mathfrak{k}}
is given by
\mathsf{g}=2(\theta^{1}\theta^{2}+\theta^{3}\theta^{4})
.

Letting
\mathrm{vol}_{\mathsf{g}}=\theta^{1}\wedge\theta^{2}\wedge\theta^{3}\wedge\theta^{4}
, the two families of totally null 2-planes for
[\mathsf{g}]
are:

•

SD:
\langle se_{1}+te_{3},se_{4}-te_{2}\rangle
, parametrized by
[s:t]\in{\mathbb{P}}^{1}
. Write
\Pi^{\mbox{\tiny SD}}_{\xi}:=\langle e_{1}+\xi e_{3},e_{4}-\xi e_{2}\rangle
and
\Pi^{\mbox{\tiny SD}}_{\xi=\infty}:=\langle e_{3},e_{2}\rangle
.

•

ASD:
\langle se_{1}+te_{4},se_{3}-te_{2}\rangle
, parametrized by
[s:t]\in{\mathbb{P}}^{1}
. Write
\Pi^{\mbox{\tiny ASD}}_{\eta}:=\langle e_{1}+\eta e_{4},e_{3}-\eta e_{2}\rangle
and
\Pi^{\mbox{\tiny ASD}}_{\eta=\infty}:=\langle e_{4},e_{2}\rangle
.

Let
{\mathfrak{k}}^{\mbox{\tiny SD}}_{\xi}
and
{\mathfrak{k}}^{\mbox{\tiny ASD}}_{\eta}
be the subalgebras of
{\mathfrak{k}}
preserving
\Pi^{\mbox{\tiny SD}}_{\xi}
and
\Pi^{\mbox{\tiny ASD}}_{\eta}
, respectively.

Fixing
\xi
and
\eta
, the 5-dimensional quotients
{\mathfrak{f}}/{\mathfrak{k}}^{\mbox{\tiny SD}}_{\xi}
and
{\mathfrak{f}}/{\mathfrak{k}}^{\mbox{\tiny ASD}}_{\eta}
are infinitesimal models that carry the associated SD and ASD twistor XXO-structures
(\ell,{\mathcal{D}})
. Let us describe the XXO-data
(\ell,{\mathcal{D}})
on them. Clearly,
{\mathfrak{k}}/{\mathfrak{k}}^{\mbox{\tiny SD}}_{\xi}\subset{\mathfrak{f}}/{\mathfrak{k}}^{\mbox{\tiny SD}}_{\xi}
and
{\mathfrak{k}}/{\mathfrak{k}}^{\mbox{\tiny ASD}}_{\eta}\subset{\mathfrak{f}}/{\mathfrak{k}}^{\mbox{\tiny ASD}}_{\eta}
generate distinguished lines
\ell
, and choosing a generator for
\ell
, we can express it as a vector field on
\xi
-space or
\eta
-space, respectively. In the SD case, the 3-plane
{\mathcal{H}}=\ell\oplus{\mathcal{D}}
is the pullback under the quotient
{\mathfrak{f}}/{\mathfrak{k}}^{\mbox{\tiny SD}}_{\xi}\to{\mathfrak{f}}/{\mathfrak{k}}
of
\Pi^{\mbox{\tiny SD}}_{\xi}
, and similarly for the ASD case. Inside
{\mathcal{H}}
, the twistor 2-plane lift
{\mathcal{D}}
is uniquely determined by
[{\mathcal{D}},{\mathcal{D}}]\subset{\mathcal{H}}
. Explicitly,

•

SD:
{\mathcal{D}}=\langle\widetilde{e}_{1}+\xi\widetilde{e}_{3}+AV,\widetilde{e}_{4}-\xi\widetilde{e}_{2}+BV\rangle\,{\rm mod}\ {\mathfrak{k}}^{\mbox{\tiny SD}}_{\xi}
, where
0\neq V\in{\mathfrak{k}}/{\mathfrak{k}}^{\mbox{\tiny SD}}_{\xi}
;

•

ASD:
{\mathcal{D}}=\langle\widetilde{e}_{1}+\eta\widetilde{e}_{4}+AV,\widetilde{e}_{3}-\eta\widetilde{e}_{2}+BV\rangle\,{\rm mod}\ {\mathfrak{k}}^{\mbox{\tiny ASD}}_{\eta}
, where
0\neq V\in{\mathfrak{k}}/{\mathfrak{k}}^{\mbox{\tiny ASD}}_{\eta}
.

Then
{\mathcal{W}}^{\pm}
are determined (up to scale) by the relative invariant
{\mathcal{I}}:\bigwedge^{2}{\mathcal{D}}\to\ell
,
(x,y)\mapsto\operatorname{proj}_{\ell}([x,y])
.

Example 5.3

(
\mathsf{M6N}
)
.

For
\mathsf{M6N}
, define
X_{1},...,X_{5},L
as in Table
6
, and
\widetilde{e}_{i},e_{i},\theta^{i}
as in Lemma
5.2
. We have
{\mathfrak{f}}^{0}=\langle f_{01}\rangle
and
{\mathfrak{k}}=\langle L,f_{01}\rangle
. The isotropy rep
\rho:{\mathfrak{k}}\to\mathfrak{gl}({\mathfrak{f}}/{\mathfrak{k}})
in the
\{e_{1},e_{2},e_{3},e_{4}\}
basis is:

(5.1)

\displaystyle\rho(c_{1}L+c_{2}f_{01})=\begin{pmatrix}-3c_{1}&0&0&0\\
0&0&6c_{1}-c_{2}&3c_{1}\\
-3c_{1}&0&0&0\\
-6c_{1}+c_{2}&0&0&-3c_{1}\end{pmatrix}.

The unique
{\mathfrak{k}}
-invariant conformal structure
[\mathsf{g}]
on
{\mathfrak{f}}/{\mathfrak{k}}
is given by

g=2\theta^{1}\theta^{2}+2\theta^{3}\theta^{4}\in S^{2}({\mathfrak{f}}/{\mathfrak{k}})^{*}
.

SD case
:
c_{1}L+c_{2}f_{01}\in{\mathfrak{k}}
acts on
\Pi^{\mbox{\tiny SD}}_{\xi}
via the infinitesimal change
\xi\mapsto 3c_{1}(\xi-1)
, i.e.
3c_{1}(\xi-1)\partial_{\xi}
:

(5.2)

\displaystyle e_{1}+\xi e_{3}\mapsto e^{t\rho(c_{1}L+c_{2}f_{01})}(e_{1}+\xi e_{3})

\displaystyle=e_{1}+\xi e_{3}+t\rho(c_{1}L+c_{2}f_{01})+O(t^{2})

(5.3)

\displaystyle\equiv(1-3c_{1}t)e_{1}+(\xi-3c_{1}t)e_{3}+O(t^{2})\,\,\,{\rm mod}\ \{e_{2},e_{4}\}

Thus,
\xi\mapsto\frac{\xi-3c_{1}t}{1-3c_{1}t}+O(t^{2})
. Differentiating at
t=0
yields
\xi\mapsto 3c_{1}(\xi-1)
. So
{\mathfrak{k}}^{\mbox{\tiny SD}}_{\xi}=\langle f_{01}\rangle={\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
, and
L
quotients to a generator of
\ell={\mathfrak{k}}/{\mathfrak{k}}^{\mbox{\tiny SD}}_{\xi}
, acting via
3(\xi-1)\partial_{\xi}
. The condition

(5.4)

\displaystyle v:=[\widetilde{e}_{1}+\xi\widetilde{e}_{3}+AL,\widetilde{e}_{4}-\xi\widetilde{e}_{2}+BL]\in\langle\widetilde{e}_{1}+\xi\widetilde{e}_{3},\widetilde{e}_{4}-\xi\widetilde{e}_{2},L,f_{01}\rangle

uniquely determines the twistor distribution
{\mathcal{D}}=\langle\widetilde{e}_{1}+\xi\widetilde{e}_{3}+AL,\widetilde{e}_{4}-\xi\widetilde{e}_{2}+BL\rangle\,{\rm mod}\ {\mathfrak{f}}^{0}
, and pins down
(A,B)=(-2\xi,0)
. The components of
v
in the basis

(5.5)

\displaystyle\widetilde{e}_{1}+\xi\widetilde{e}_{3}-2\xi L,\quad\widetilde{e}_{4}-\xi\widetilde{e}_{2},\quad L,\quad f_{01}

are
(-2\xi+2,4,-2(\xi-1)^{2},16\xi^{2})
. The projection
\bigwedge^{2}{\mathcal{D}}\to\ell
has image a multiple of
-2(\xi-1)^{2}L
. But we saw that
L
acts via
3(\xi-1)\partial_{\xi}
, so
{\mathcal{W}}^{+}
is a multiple of
(\xi-1)^{3}
. Viewing this as a quartic on
\xi\in{\mathbb{P}}^{1}
, it is Petrov type
\mathsf{III}
, with a triple root at
\xi=1
and a simple root at
\xi=\infty
. At these exceptional values, we get integrable 2-planes
\langle\widetilde{e}_{1}+\widetilde{e}_{3},\widetilde{e}_{4}-\widetilde{e}_{2}\rangle\,{\rm mod}\ {\mathfrak{k}}^{\mbox{\tiny SD}}_{\xi=1}
and
\langle\widetilde{e}_{3},\widetilde{e}_{2}\rangle\,{\rm mod}\ {\mathfrak{k}}^{\mbox{\tiny SD}}_{\xi=\infty}
.

ASD case
:
c_{1}L+c_{2}f_{01}\in{\mathfrak{k}}
acts on
\Pi^{\mbox{\tiny ASD}}_{\eta}
via
(c_{2}-6c_{1})\partial_{\eta}
, so
{\mathfrak{k}}^{\mbox{\tiny ASD}}_{\eta}=\langle L+6f_{01}\rangle
, and
L
quotients to a generator of
\ell={\mathfrak{k}}/{\mathfrak{k}}^{\mbox{\tiny ASD}}_{\eta}
, acting via
-6\partial_{\eta}
. We now proceed similarly as above.

Data associated to all our (complex) models is compiled below.
